L839: **Table 50. Twenty-Six-Week Monkey Study Observations and Results**
L840: Parameter	Major Findings
Mortality	
There were two unscheduled deaths during the Treatment Period.
One female animal (Animal #537) in the 200 mg/kg/week SC group was euthanized for welfare reasons on Day 66 (Week 10). The Applicant's report stated that Animal #537 exhibited rapid development of a large swelling starting on the right-hand mandible, extending across the right-hand side of the throat and down the right shoulder immediately prior to termination. The animal also had clinical signs of a distinct raspiness on vocalization, an elevated body temperature (i.e., 39.3°C), and its breathing was reportedly slightly pyrexic. The animal exhibited macroscopic abnormalities of dark colored lungs, discolored (pale and dark areas) and enlarged kidneys, abnormal shape of the gallbladder, enlarged mandibular lymph node, raised areas of the skeletal muscle, thyroids cysts, edematous mandibular salivary gland, and dark areas at the subcutaneous injection sites. This animal exhibited histopathological findings of marked myofiber necrosis and slight regeneration of the skeletal muscle, minimal plasmacytosis of the mandibular lymph node, minimal inflammatory cell infiltration of the mandibular and sublingual salivary glands and slight hemorrhage and inflammatory cell infiltration of the subcutaneous injection sites which correlated with observed macroscopic findings. This animal was confirmed as ADA-positive.
One male animal (Animal #238) in the 100 mg/kg/week IV group was euthanized on Day 119 (Week 17) due to a sudden onset of abdominal swelling, swollen muzzle (which was considered severe enough to necessitate euthanasia), and subcutaneous fluid accumulation. The Applicant's report stated that a postmortem examination revealed edematous adipose tissue, pancreas, salivary glands, and skin/subcutis, and gastrointestinal findings of thickened jejunum and duodenum with red mucosa of the jejunum. This animal exhibited histopathological findings of edema in the pancreas and inflammatory cell infiltration of the heart, kidneys, esophagus,
L841: Parameter	Major Findings
mandibular and sublingual salivary glands. This animal was ADA-negative following a confirmatory analysis. The Applicant's report stated that the major factor leading to the decision to euthanize this animal was the marked clinical observations.
Clinical signs	No treatment-related effects.
Body weights	No treatment-related effects.
Ophthalmoscopy	No treatment-related effects.
ECG	No treatment-related effects on ECG, blood pressure, respiration rate, or pulse rate.
Hematology	
An increase in neutrophil counts was observed for males (up to 92%) in the 60 and 200 mg/kg SC treatment groups relative to control animals at the end of the 26-week Treatment Period. Clear dose-dependent effects were not observed. These findings were not considered adverse and were considered monitorable in a clinical setting.
A slight decrease in red blood cell (up to 7%) and hematocrit (up to 6%) levels were observed for females in the 200 mg/kg SC treatment group relative to control animals. The findings were not considered adverse and are judged to be monitorable in a clinical setting.

Coagulation	
A significant dose-dependent prolongation of activated partial thromboplastin times (aPTT) were observed for males and females in all intravenous and subcutaneous treatment groups relative to control animals during the 26-week Treatment Period. On Day 1, aPTT prolongation times for the IV treatment groups peaked at either 0.25 or 5 h postdose (i.e., males up to 3.5-fold and females up to 3.2-fold the control) and remained elevated up to 168 h postdose. In addition, aPTT was also prolonged in the subcutaneous treatment groups and peaked at 96 and 48 h postdose in males (up to 2.5-fold the control) and females (up to 3.1-fold the control), respectively, and remained extended up to 168 h postdose. The findings were an expected pharmacological effect of the drug. The findings were not completely reversible by the end of the 8-week recovery period.
No treatment-related effects on prothrombin times were observed during the study period.

Clinical chemistry	A slight increase in potassium concentration was observed for females (up to 27%) in the 60 and 200 mg/kg SC treatment groups relative to control animals. Clear dose-dependent effects were not observed. The toxicological significance, if any, of these findings is unclear. These findings were considered monitorable in a clinical setting.
Urinalysis	A significant decrease in potassium (up to 48%) and chloride (up to 70%) levels were observed for females in the 200 mg/kg/week SC treatment group relative to control animals at the end of the 26-week Treatment Period. The toxicological significance, if any, of these findings is unclear. These findings were considered clinically monitorable.
Gross pathology	Dark areas and thickening observed at the subcutaneous or intravenous injections sites were observed for several animals at the end of the Treatment Period. These findings were not considered toxicologically significant.
Organ weights	No treatment-related effects.
L842: Parameter	Major Findings
Histopathology
Adequate battery: Yes	
The potential target organs of toxicity were identified as the subcutaneous and intravenous injection sites.
An increased incidence and/or severity of minimal to slight subcutaneous hemorrhage and minimal to slight inflammatory cell infiltration at the subcutaneous injection sites (sites 1 and 2) were observed for males and females in the 200 mg/kg SC treatment groups relative to control animals. These findings were considered test article related. These findings were reversible.
Minimal to slight lymphoid aggregates at the subcutaneous injection sites (sites 1 and 2) was observed for males and females in the 200 mg/kg SC treatment groups. These findings were considered test article related. The findings were not completely reversible at the end of the 8-week recovery period.
An increased incidence and/or severity of minimal to slight subcutaneous hemorrhage, minimal to slight inflammatory cell infiltration, and minimal dermal inflammatory cell infiltration at the venous injection site (left) was observed for females in the 100 mg/kg IV treatment group relative to control animals. These findings were reversible.
The injection site findings were not considered adverse and are clinically monitorable.

Other evaluations	No treatment-related effects on complement C3a, D-dimer concentrations, thrombin-antithrombin complex, C-reactive protein, cytokine release, and immunophenotyping of lymphocytes.
Toxicokinetics	Systemic exposure (C_{max} and AUC_{0-168}) to garadacimab generally increased in a dose proportional manner for males and females from 10 to 100 mg/kg IV and 60 to 200 mg/kg SC during the 26-week Treatment Period. Moderate accumulation of garadacimab was observed in the dosing groups during the study period (i.e., 1-4-fold). No clear differences in systemic exposure between males and females were observed. T_{1/2} ranged from 167 to 211 h (Week 14) for IV administration and 165 to 208 h (Week 14) for SC administration. Toxicokinetic data are summarized in Table 51.

L865: ## 13.1.4.2. Carcinogenicity
L866: An assessment of the carcinogenic potential associated with chronic inhibition of FXIIa by garadacimab was conducted by the Applicant and the Nonclinical Reviewers.
L867: - The mouse was demonstrated to be a pharmacologically relevant species with garadacimab. A 4-week study with garadacimab was conducted in the mouse; however, this study showed that mice developed an immunogenic response to treatment with this heterologous humanized monoclonal antibody. Therefore, a 2-year mouse carcinogenicity study was not considered technically feasible.
- A 2-year carcinogenicity study in rats would not provide meaningful information for the safety assessment of garadacimab as the rat was not a pharmacologically relevant species (i.e., no binding of garadacimab to rat FXIIa).
L868: - The 26-week toxicity study in monkeys did not show any evidence of preneoplastic or neoplastic findings.
- The evidence from the published scientific literature regarding inhibition of activated FXII or zymogen FXII deficiency does not suggest any carcinogenic risk. Evidence suggests that inflammatory responses and possible maintenance and progression of cancer could be reduced by inhibition of FXIIa.
- There is general agreement that hereditary FXII deficiency does not usually result in a hemorrhagic diathesis. FXII knock-out mice are not impacted negatively, although zymogen FXII has biologic activity in various cells in vivo.
- FXII plasma levels are low in patients with lung, gastrointestinal, and colorectal cancer, suggesting that FXIIa-driven pathways are active in patients with malignancy. Experimental venous thromboembolism models in mice and studies with cancer patients revealed that prostate cancer cells and associated exosomes display inorganic polymer polyphosphates on their plasma membranes. Polyphosphates activate FXII and initiate thrombus formation. Pharmacologic inhibition of FXIIa protects mice from venous thromboembolism and reduces thrombin coagulant activity in plasma of patients with prostate cancer.
- Garadacimab was shown to only antagonize FXIIa enzymatic activities, displaying 100% inhibition of FXIIa catalytic activity for both the \alpha and \beta fragments, meaning that zymogen FXII functions are negligibly impacted. In contrast, garadacimab did not significantly inhibit the other serum proteases evaluated. Due to its specific binding to FXIIa without impacting other coagulation factors or serum proteases, it was considered unlikely that garadacimab will induce preneoplastic or neoplastic lesions.
L869: Based on the weight of evidence, the Division agreed with the Applicant that a rodent carcinogenicity study was not technically feasible. Further, there appears to be a low or negligible carcinogenic potential based on the results of the 26-week monkey study and review of the published literature regarding chronic inhibition of FXIIa.
L870: Genotoxicity studies with biologics such as garadacimab are not required. As per International Council for Harmonisation (ICH) guideline, S6(R1) *Preclinical Safety Evaluation of Biotechnology-Derived Pharmaceuticals* (June 2011), standard in vitro and in vivo genotoxicity studies are not applicable to biologics because these molecules are unlikely to interact directly with DNA or chromosomal material.
L871: ## 13.1.4.3. Reproductive Toxicology
L872: The potential reproductive toxicity of garadacimab was evaluated in fertility and early embryonic development studies with male and female rabbits, an embryofetal development study with female rabbits, and a pre- and postnatal development study with female rabbits.
L873: Rabbits were determined to be a pharmacologically relevant species.
L874: - Garadacimab binds with high affinity to rabbit FXIIa and \betaFXIIa as well as FXII zymogen. Data demonstrated that rabbits are a pharmacologically relevant species for toxicological investigation (REP-21280).
L875: - Garadacimab inhibited dextran sulfate-mediated FXIIa activity in diluted plasma from rabbit, human, and other animal species confirming rabbits as a pharmacologically relevant species and supporting their use in toxicological investigations (REP-21282).
L876: In correspondence dated March 29, 2019, the Division agreed with the Applicant's reproductive toxicity program. Rats were not a pharmacologically relevant species.
L877: Rabbits are not commonly used in male and female fertility studies.
L878: - The duration of the spermatogenic cycle is 48 days in the rabbit as compared to 52 days in rats, which are more commonly used in fertility studies. Treatment of rabbits with garadacimab once every 3 days for a total of 15 doses should cover the majority of the duration of the spermatogenic cycle in rabbits. Rabbits were not used in general toxicology studies with garadacimab, so there were no microscopic evaluations of the testes in longer studies. The assessment of fertility using male rabbits appears to be generally acceptable as the fertility study covered the majority of the spermatogenic cycle.
- Female rabbits differ from female humans in that they do not have an estrus cycle. Females ovulate once they are mounted by a male (induced ovulation). The female rabbit can become impregnated any time, although it is not recommended that females be bred before 4 weeks of age. The assessment of fertility using female rabbits appears to be generally acceptable, although potential effects of estrus cycle could not be determined.
L879: Rabbits are commonly used in embryofetal development studies.
L880: Pre-and postnatal development studies are not commonly conducted with female rabbits. The Applicant appropriately adjusted dosing to cover the duration of the gestation and lactation periods in rabbits. Monitoring of the growth and development of the F1 generation was also appropriately adjusted for the use of rabbits.
L881: ## **Fertility and Early Embryonic Development Studies Conducted With Male and Female Rabbits**
L882: The 26-week repeat-dose monkey study was conducted in sexually immature male and female animals, and estrous cycling was not monitored in females. Therefore, an assessment of the effects of garadacimab on male and female fertility was not possible from this study. The Applicant conducted fertility and early embryonic development studies with male and female rabbits.
L883: ## **Intravenous Injection Male Fertility and Early Embryonic Development to Implantation and Toxicokinetics Study With CSL312 in Rabbits (Study # [REDACTED](b)(4))**
L884: Good laboratory practice (GLP) compliance: Yes
L885: ## **Key Study Findings**
L886: - In this study, male rabbits were administered IV doses of 10, 30, or 100 mg/kg/dose every 3 days from 4 weeks prior to mating, during the mating period, and up to 8 days after the mating period (dosing on Preparing Days 0, 3, 6, 9, 12, 15, 18, 21, 24, and 27; Pairing Days 2 and 5; and on Postpairing Days 2, 5, and 8 for a total of 15 doses). Females were not dosed and assigned to each group for mating purposes only.
L887: - Six males (four LD and two MD males) died or were sacrificed prior to the scheduled sacrifice. However, there were questionable relationships of these early mortalities to treatment with garadacimab based on observed lack of dose-response with no deaths in the HD group. The role of ADAs in these deaths was unclear.
- Expected garadacimab pharmacologically related prolonged aPTT was observed in all drug-treated groups.
- There were no treatment-related effects on mating and fertility parameters, reproductive indices, or sperm assessment.
- Prolonged aPTT and TK indicated that drug-treated males were continuously exposed to drug from 4 weeks prior to dosing through Postpairing Day 8.
- The NOAEL for this study was the HD of 100 mg/kg/dose based on a lack of findings.
L888: **Table 52. Methods of Male Rabbit Fertility and Early Embryonic Development Study**
L889: Parameter	Method Details
Dose and frequency of dosing:	10 (LD), 30 (MD), and 100 (HD) mg/kg/dose
Male rabbits were administered IV doses of 10, 30, or 100 mg/kg/dose every 3 days from 4 weeks prior to mating, during the mating period, and up to 8 days after the mating period (dosing on Preparing Days 0, 3, 6, 9, 12, 15, 18, 21, 24, and 27; Pairing Days 2 and 5; and on Postpairing Days 2, 5, and 8 for a total of 15 doses) by IV prior to mating and until termination. Females were not dosed and assigned to each group for mating purposes only.
Route of administration:	Intravenous
Formulation/vehicle:	Drug formulation: 20mM L-histidine, 150mM (b) L-arginine monohydrochloride, 140mM L-proline, 0.02% w/v polysorbate 80; pH 6.1
Control: 0.9% sodium chloride for injection
Species/strain:	Rabbit
Hra:(NZW)SPF
Males were received from [REDACTED] (b) (4). At initiation of dosing, males were 7 to 8 months old, and body weights ranged from 2908 to 4102 g.

L902: ## Intravenous Injection Female Fertility and Early Embryonic Development to Implantation and Toxicokinetics Study With CSL312 in Rabbits (Study # [REDACTED] (b) (4))

L904: ## Key Study Findings
L905: - In this study, female rabbits were administered IV doses of 10, 30, or 100 mg/kg/dose every 3 days over 2 weeks prior to mating, during the mating period, and up to Gestation Day 7 (dosed on Premating Days 0, 3, 6, 9, and 12; Pairing Days 1 and 4 (unless mating was confirmed beforehand); and on Gestation Days 1, 4, and 7 for a total of eight to ten administrations, depending on mating status). For females without evidence of mating, dosing continued after completion of the 7-day pairing phase, on Postpairing Days 1, 4, and 7. Males were not dosed and were assigned to each group for mating purposes only.
L906: - No garadacimab-related clinical observations, or changes in female body weight or body weight gain were noted.
- No garadacimab-related effects were noted on mating index, fecundity index, or fertility index. The fertility rates for drug-treated females in the control, LD, MD, and HD groups were 96, 92, 96, and 92%, respectively.
- No garadacimab-related effects on maternal reproductive parameters or embryo survival were observed.
- Prolonged aPTT and TK indicated that drug-treated females were continuously exposed to drug from 2 weeks prior to mating through Gestation Day 7. Prolongation of aPTT was not associated with any bleeding events.
- The NOAEL for maternal and developmental toxicity and female fertility was the HD, 100 mg/kg/dose.

L916: ## CSL312: Study for Effects on Embryo-Fetal Development in the New Zealand White Rabbit by Intravenous (Bolus) Administration (Study # (b) (4))
L917: GLP Compliance: Yes
L918: ## Key Study Findings
L919: - In this study, pregnant rabbits were administered IV doses of 10, 30, and 100 mg/kg/occasion on Gestation Days 6, 9, 12, 15 and 18. Necropsies were conducted on Day 29 after mating.
- Two HD animals were euthanized prematurely due to welfare reasons. A relationship of the two early deaths to garadacimab treatment could not be completely excluded; however, it seems unlikely given no mortality in the fertility and early embryonic development and pre- and postnatal development studies with an identical IV dose of 100 mg/kg.
L920: - As expected for the pharmacological mechanism of action of garadacimab, an increase in aPTT was observed during treatment. The increase persisted until the end of the observation period (11 days after cessation of treatment). The aPTT prolongation suggested FXIIa inhibition at all dose levels tested. Prolongation of aPTT was not associated with any bleeding events.
- Systemic drug exposures were continuous during the period of organogenesis from Gestation Days 6 to 18 based on TK and aPTT data.
- There was no evidence of maternal toxicity related to garadacimab treatment. There were no effects of garadacimab treatment of does on F1 offspring at all dose levels tested (e.g., no evidence of fetal harm). There were no treatment-related effects on fetal body weights and placental weights.
- There were no treatment-related external, visceral, or skeletal major abnormalities.
- Increased numbers of thoracolumbar vertebrae (20), a minor abnormality, were observed for fetuses in all garadacimab-treated groups. This finding was not reported in the laboratory historical control data.
- The NOAEL for maternal and fetal toxicity was concluded to be 100 mg/kg/occasion (HD).

Study design:	
Age of the animals at the start of the study (Day 0 after mating): 18 to 24 weeks old. Weight range of the animals at the start of the study (Day 0 after mating): 2.45 to 4.28 kg
Basis of dose selection and dosing frequency: A preliminary embryofetal development study (# [REDACTED] (b) (4)) was conducted in pregnant rabbits with garadacimab. Animals were dosed with garadacimab once every 3 days on Gestation Days 6, 9, 12, 15 and 18 at doses of 1, 10 and 100 mg/kg and showed no adverse findings. The dose of 100 mg/kg was selected for the preliminary study as it was the dose for toxicity studies in mice, rabbits, and monkeys with no adverse findings. In a prior FDA review for IND 139936 dated March 25, 2019 by Dr. Brett Jones, the summary of the preliminary study was reviewed.
The HD represents a 10-fold margin of the maximum intended clinical IV dose (10 mg/kg). Per ICH S5(R3), the dose should provide an approximately 10-fold exposure multiple over the maximum exposure to be achieved in the clinic.

L925: **Table 57. Observations and Results from Rabbit Embryonic and Fetal Development Study**
L926: Parameter	Major Findings
Mortality	
Two HD animals were euthanized prematurely due to welfare reasons.
Animal #78 was killed on Gestation Day 16. Clinical signs comprised thin build, loose feces, fecal fur staining and little or no diet eaten. Necropsy examination revealed ten live embryos.
Animal #69 was killed on Gestation Day 22. Clinical signs comprised little hay and food eaten, little water drunk, thin build, reduced fecal output, and red/pink staining on the tray paper. Necropsy examination revealed eight late resorptions and no live embryos; the red/pink staining on the undertray was considered to be due to the commencement of the expulsion of the fetal material.
Decreased food consumption and disturbances of fecal output were evident in both HD females. It is noted that pregnant rabbits are sensitive to experimental manipulations that can result in gut disturbances, inappetence and subsequent abortions (Cappon et al. 2005). There was no evidence of toxicity for the F0 females in the FEED and PPND studies with an identical IV dose of 100 mg/kg administered once every 3 or 5 days. It is difficult to completely exclude a treatment relationship for the deaths of the 2 HD does; however, a relationship seems unlikely.

L931: ## Excerpted from Applicant's submission
L932: ADA	
The ADA results were inconclusive as the control animals were tested positive. In the control, 4/22 (approx. 18.2%) animals tested positive on Day 19, and 3 of these 4 animals remained positive on Day 29. These results are considered false positive due to unknown reasons, as no garadacimab was detected in plasma (toxicokinetic) samples taken from the control. garadacimab plasma concentration at the time of ADA sampling remained above the drug tolerance level in the assay system. Since exposure to garadacimab and pharmacodynamic activity (i.e., aPTT prolongation) was confirmed throughout the study, potential ADA formation was not considered to have impacted the outcome of the study.

aPTT	
Mean aPTT values determined approximately 5 min after the first injection were more than three-fold higher in garadacimab-treated animals than in the control. The mean aPTT on Day 18 (approximately 5 min after the fifth and final administration) showed mean values approximately 4- to 5-fold higher than the control. On Day 29 after mating (11 days after the last dose), mean aPTT values of treated animals remained approximately 4- to 6-fold higher than the control. There was no clear dose-relationship shown.
L933: Parameter	Major Findings
Based on an in vitro spike study (# (b) (4)) performed in the same laboratory (garadacimab spiked in rabbit plasma followed by aPTT determination) and the doses/dosing regimen used in the present study, the lack of dose-dependency in aPTT was expected since maximum FXIIa inhibition and respective aPTT prolongation would be expected at all doses. Prolongation of aPTT was not associated with any bleeding events.

L937: ## CSL312: A Pre- and Postnatal Development Study Including Toxicokinetics Following a Subcutaneous or Intravenous Injection in Rabbits (Study #8410864)
L938: GLP Compliance: Yes
L939: ## Key Study Findings
L940: - In this study, pregnant rabbits (F0) were administered garadacimab doses of 0, 10 (LD), 30 (MD), and 100 (HD) mg/kg/dose by SC, and 100 (HD) mg/kg/dose by IV, once every 5 days from implantation (Gestation Day 7) through weaning (Lactation Day 38). F0 toxicity animals were dosed on Gestation Day 7, 12, 17, 22, and 27 and Lactation Days 3, 8, 13, 18, 23, 28, 33, and 38. F0 placental transfer animals were dosed on Gestation Day 7, 12, 17, 22, and 27. The F1 generation were not directly administered the test article.
- For F0 dams, no garadacimab-related effects were observed on mortality, clinical signs, body weight, natural delivery, litter data, or maternal macroscopic observations.
- For the F1 generation, no garadacimab-related effects were observed on mortality, clinical signs, body weight, developmental signs, functional observation battery, eye blink response, presence of motile sperm, reproductive performance, cesarean section parameters, or preweaning or postweaning macroscopic observations.
- Garadacimab-related prolongation in aPTT was observed in all treated F0 females. It was an expected pharmacological effect of garadacimab.For TK, the increases in exposures (mean C_{max} and AUC0-120 values) were generally less than dose proportional in dose levels from 10 to 100 mg/kg SC. Accumulation was observed after multiple SC and IV doses. Fetal to maternal concentration ratios ranged from 2% to 6.5% following SC doses and 2.8% following IV doses. On Gestation
L941: Day 29, fetal parturition plasma garadacimab concentrations were 76.6%, 136.6%, and 40.8% of the maternal concentrations, at SC LD, SC MD, and SC HD, respectively. At IV HD, maternal and fetal garadacimab concentrations were approximately equivalent.

- Prolongation of aPTT values and TK indicated that drug exposure was continuous during the gestation and lactation periods in F0 maternal animals.
- Maternal ADA incidence was 43.6% (17 of 39), 16.7% (7 of 42), and 11.1% (5 of 45) for SC LD, SC MD, and SC HD, respectively, and 6.8% (3 of 44) for IV HD. ADA was all negative for control maternal animals. The SC LD appeared to have a higher incidence of maternal positive ADA induction.
- The maternal NOAEL is 100 mg/kg/dose (HD) SC/IV. In the F1 generation, the NOAEL for fetal development is 100 mg/kg/dose (HD) SC/IV. The NOAEL for developmental, neurobehavioral, and reproductive performance in the F1 generation is 100 mg/kg/dose (HD) SC/IV.

L944: Parameter	Method Details
Study design:	
Basis of dose selection:
The dose levels for this pre- and postnatal developmental toxicity study were determined based upon the previously conducted male and female fertility studies with garadacimab (Study (b) (4) and Study (b) (4)). The male and female fertility studies were conducted in NZW rabbits at dose levels of 10, 30, and 100 mg/kg administered once every 3 days via IV injection. No garadacimab-related paternal or maternal mortality occurred. No garadacimab-related clinical observations or effects on mean body weight or mean food consumption were observed. Mating and fertility indices in both sexes were not affected by garadacimab. Dose levels for this prenatal and postnatal developmental toxicity study were 10, 30, or 100 mg/kg once every 5 days. The dosing frequency was changed from once every 3 days to once every 5 days.

Deviation from study protocol affecting interpretation of results:	None that affected the integrity of the study

L951: ## 13.1.4.4. Other Toxicology/Specialized Studies
L952: ## Cross-Reactivity Study in Human Tissues and Selective Cynomolgus Monkey Tissues
L953: ## (Study # (b) (4))
L954: The aim of this GLP study was to assess the potential cross-reactivity of garadacimab with specified tissues from human and cynomolgus monkey origin. Garadacimab (5, 2.5, and 1.25 µg/mL) and an isotype control antibody (5 and 2.5 µg/mL) were applied to a full panel of normal human and cynomolgus monkey tissues. Specific staining was observed with garadacimab in many human tissues; with strongest staining intensities observed in liver, bone marrow, lung, spinal cord, cerebral cortex, and eye (optic nerve). No staining was observed with garadacimab on human ovary, cervix, striated muscle, colon, ileum, thyroid, and endothelium. Specific staining in cynomolgus monkeys was predictive of staining in human tissues, with the exception of parathyroid, where only specific staining was observed in human tissues.
L955: ## Local Tolerance and Bioequivalence Study of Two CSL312 Formulations Following
L956: ## Subcutaneous Administration in Rabbits (Study # (b) (4))
L957: ## **Key Study Findings**
L958: - In the GLP-compliant local tolerance study, two groups (3 sex/group, around 5 weeks old) of New Zealand white rabbits were treated with two formulations of garadacimab (batch no. 834101076 or batch no. 8120000014) SC on the right side (dorsal skin) of the animals on test days 1 and 8. All animals were treated with 0.9% NaCl solution as negative control on the left side. Following an observation period of 11 days after administration, all animals were sacrificed and the injection sites were examined macro- and microscopically.
- There were no signs of toxicity evident from clinical signs, body weights, macroscopic changes, and microscopic changes.
- In group 1, lymphohistiocytic infiltration (3/3 males), neutrophilic granulocytes in the subcutis (2/3 males), and hemorrhage (3/3 females) were noted on the right side (garadacimab-treated). In group 2, lymphohistiocytic infiltration (1/3 males, 1/3 females), neutrophilic granulocytes in the subcutis (1/3 males, 1/3 females), and hemorrhage (1/3 females) were noted on the right side (garadacimab treated).
- The two formulations showed similar PK profiles.
- Repeated SC administration of two formulations of garadacimab on 2 days was well tolerated and did not reveal any test item-related changes at the injection sites.
L959: ## Extractables and Leachables Studies With the Container Closure System
L960: Garadacimab was developed as a combination product incorporating a prefilled syringe which contains 200 mg of the formulated garadacimab active substance, defined as the garadacimab seminished product 200 mg. Extractables and leachables studies were conducted with the container closure system which were evaluated (see review dated June 5, 2024). (b) (4)
L961: . The levels of leachables from the primary container closure system of garadacimab
L962: semifinished product 200 mg were judged to pose no safety concerns. The assessment was not ideal as only one batch was evaluated.

L989: The proposed to-be-marketed (TBM) drug products for SC administration include a 200 mg/1.2 mL prefilled syringe with needle safety device (PFS-NSD) and a 200 mg/1.2 mL autoinjector (AI). The TBM formulation is a solution with a garadacimab concentration of 170 mg/mL. The TBM formulation in the PFS-NSD presentation was used in studies 1003, 1004, and 3002 and trial 3001. The AI presentation was used in studies 1004 and 3002.
L990: Across all clinical studies, two drug product formulations have been evaluated, including a 100 mg/mL solution formulation (vial presentation), and the TBM 170 mg/mL solution formulation. The two formulations contain the same excipients at the same concentration and only differ in garadacimab concentration. The 100 mg/mL formulation was used in studies 1001, and 1003 (IV arms only) and trial 2001. Direct clinical data comparing garadacimab exposure following administration of the two formulations are not available. The Applicant's rationale to support equivalence of the formulations is based on the following:
L991: - A single dose nonclinical PK study in rabbits comparing the two formulations at a dose of 20 mg/kg showing a similar PK profile.
- A comparison of the steady state trough concentration across trials 2001 (100 mg/mL) and 3001 and study 3002 (170 mg/mL) using population PK indicated that formulation has no impact on garadacimab PK.
L992: Garadacimab steady state trough concentrations following a 200-mg dose, stratified by formulation, are shown in Figure 20. The data show that the median steady state trough concentrations are slightly higher using the 170 mg/mL formulation (visually estimated at about 7500 ng/mL) relative to the 100 mg/mL formulation (visually estimated at about 7000 ng/mL). However, there is large overlap in the distributions based on the observed data.
L993: The mean (SD) steady-state trough concentrations in trial 2001 among subjects who were randomized to receive 200 mg SC every 4 weeks (Q4W) ranged from 6414 (2728) to 7001 (1715) ng/mL in Treatment Period 1; and 5718 (2271) to 7656 (2728) ng/mL in Treatment Period 2 (data derived from Table 14.2.2.6.4, Clinical Study Report [CSR]) for trial 2001). Mean (SD) steady state trough concentrations in trial 3001 ranged from 8087 (4277) to 8692 (3934) ng/mL. Mean (SD) steady-state trough concentrations in study 3002 ranged from 7429 (4759) to 7882 (4276) ng/mL. Overall, garadacimab trough concentrations at steady state overlap between the 100 mg/mL formulation and the 170 mg/mL formulation.

L996: ## 14.2.1. Study 1004 (Relative Bioavailability Study Comparing PFS-NSD and AI)
L997: ## Title
L998: A phase 1, randomized, open-label, parallel-group study to compare the PK properties of garadacimab administered by SC prefilled syringe assembled to AI to prefilled syringe assembled to needle safety device in healthy adult subjects.
L999: ## Objectives
L1000: Primary objectives of the study were to characterize and compare the PK of a single SC injection of garadacimab administered via garadacimab AI or garadacimab PFS-NSD. Safety and tolerability were also assessed as secondary objectives.
L1001: ## **Study Design**
L1002: Study 1004 was designed as an open-label, parallel-group study in healthy adult subjects (Figure 21). Subjects were stratified by body weight (i.e., &lt;80 kg or \ge80 kg) and then randomized 1:1:1:1:1:1 to one of six groups defined based on device (i.e., PFS-NSD or AI) and injection site (i.e., abdomen, thigh, or upper arm). All subjects received a single 200 mg SC dose of garadacimab.
L1003: The study enrolled male or female subjects aged 18 to 55 years, with body weight between 50 and 100 kg, inclusive, and body mass index between 18 and 30 kg/m2, inclusive. The only permitted therapies included hormonal contraceptives, as required; therapies to treat adverse events (AEs); and all non-live vaccines, including vaccines against COVID-19. Administration of any other investigational agent within five half-lives of 30 days, whichever was longer, before administration of garadacimab was prohibited during the study.
L1004: **Figure 21. Study Design, Study 1004**
L1005: CSL312 AI = prefilled CSL312 syringe assembled with an autoinjector; CSL312 NSD = prefilled CSL312 syringe assembled with a needle safety device; N = total number of subjects; SC = subcutaneous.
L1006: Source: Figure 9-1, Clinical Study Report for study 1004.
Abbreviations: CSL312, garadacimab
L1007: ## **PK and Immunogenicity Sampling**
L1008: Blood PK samples were collected at predose on Day 1, and postdose at Hours 8, 24, 32, and 48 and Days 5, 8, 15, 22, 29, 43, 64, and 85.
L1009: Immunogenicity samples were collected at predose on Day 1, and on Days 29 and 85.
L1010: ## **Subject Disposition and Demographics**
L1011: A total of 132 subjects were randomized to treatment and received garadacimab, including 66 subjects each who received the AI and PFS-NSD (AI: N=22 per injection site; PFS-NSD: N=21 to 23 per injection site). Overall, 131 subjects completed the study. One subject in the AI group was lost to follow-up and discontinued the study (Table 63).
L1012: The mean (SD) body weight at baseline was 73.0 (9.8) kg in the PFS-NSD group and 75.2 (11.5) kg in the AI group. In each device group, most subjects had body weight &lt;80 kg: 46/66 (70%) subjects in the AI group, and 47/66 (71%) subjects in the PFS-NSD group.

L1020: ## **PK Analysis**
L1021: The 132 subjects in the PK Analysis Set comprised those who received garadacimab and had at least one quantifiable concentration measurement. PK parameters were estimated using noncompartmental analyses. Point estimates and 90% CIs for geometric mean ratios were derived from log-transformed PK parameters.
L1022: ## **PK Results**
L1023: Mean (SD) garadacimab concentration-time profiles by device are shown in Figure 22. PK parameters by device are shown in Table 64. Statistical analyses of Cmax and AUC0-inf are shown in Table 65. Across both treatment groups, the median time to maximum concentration (Tmax) was approximately 6 days and the mean (SD) half-life was 17.4 (3.1) days. Bioequivalence
L1024: criteria were met between the PFS-NSD and the AI. The geometric mean ratios and 90% CIs for C_{max} and AUC_{0-inf} comparing the AI to the needle safety device were 0.923 (0.813, 1.05) and 0.963 (0.866, 1.07), respectively.

L1046: ## **Immunogenicity Results**

L1048: ## **14.2.2. Study 1001 (SAD Study in Healthy Subjects)**
L1049: ## **Title**
L1050: A phase 1, single-center, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, single ascending dose study to investigate the safety, tolerability, and PKs of IV and SC garadacimab in healthy subjects.
L1051: ## **Objectives**
L1052: Primary objective of this study was to assess the safety and tolerability of escalating doses of garadacimab after a single IV infusion or SC injection in healthy subjects. Characterization of PK and immunogenicity were also included as secondary objectives.
L1053: ## **Study Design**
L1054: Study 1001 was designed as a single-center, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, single ascending dose study in healthy adult subjects (Figure 24). All subjects received a single IV or SC dose of garadacimab and were followed until 85 days postdose. A total of eight cohorts (n=6/cohort; 4 active, 2 placebo) were enrolled, including five IV cohorts in which subjects received placebo or garadacimab at doses of 0.1, 0.3, 1, 3, or 10 mg/kg, and three SC cohorts in which subjects received placebo or garadacimab at doses of 1, 3, or 10 mg/kg. Both IV and SC
L1055: doses were administered using a 100 mg/mL solution formulation in a vial presentation. IV doses were given as an infusion at a volume of 60 mL over 60 min.
L1056: The study enrolled male subjects aged 18 to 45 years, with body mass index between 18 and &lt;30 \text{ kg/m}^2. Use of prescription or over-the-counter medications, including herbal products and dietary supplements, anticoagulant therapy or drugs with antiplatelet action were prohibited within 23 days before administration of the investigational product. All medications were prohibited during the study except for acetaminophen.

L1076: ## PK Analysis
L1077: The 48 subjects in the PK analysis set comprised those who received one dose of garadacimab and for whom at least one concentration value was reported. PK parameters were estimated using noncompartmental analyses.
L1078: ## PK Results
L1079: Note that the following PK results from study 1001 should be interpreted with caution due to limitations of in-study analysis. In each of 14 runs (~10% of passing runs), the mean concentration for one of the quality control (QC) levels fell outside the acceptance criteria. Replicate QC data were not provided. Therefore, the reviewer is unable to verify whether the acceptance criteria were met based on at least 50% of QCs per QC level falling within \pm 20\% of nominal values (Table 91). The impact to the reported PK results is unclear. Therefore, results should be interpreted with caution.
L1080: Mean (SD) garadacimab concentration-time profiles by dose group for the IV and SC cohorts are shown in Figure 25 and Figure 26, respectively. PK parameters by dose and route of administration are shown in Table 69. Following single IV infusions of garadacimab, plasma concentrations generally peaked at the end of infusion, approximately 1 h. However, the median Tmax in the 0.1 mg/kg IV cohort was approximately 3.5 h. In SC cohorts, the median Tmax ranged from 5.5 to 7 days. Across all IV and SC cohorts, the mean half-life ranged from 14 to 20 days.

L1092: Among the IV cohorts, Cmax increased approximately 4-, 12-, 40-, and 150-fold with a 3-, 10-, 30-, and 100-fold increase in dose from 0.1 to 0.3, 1, 3, and 10 mg/kg, respectively. Mean AUC0-t increased 5-, 12-, 41-, and 131-fold with 3-, 10-, 30-, and 100-fold increases in dose. In SC cohorts, Cmax increased approximately 3- and 6-fold with 3- and 10-fold increases in dose from 1 to 3 and 10 mg/kg, respectively. Mean AUC0-t increased 3- and 7-fold with 3- and 10-fold increases in dose. The Applicant assessed dose proportionality of garadacimab Cmax, AUC0-inf, and AUC0-t using a Power Law model. Slopes and 90% CIs were estimated using body weight adjusted doses and total doses. Data for IV and SC cohorts are shown in Table 70 and Table 71, respectively. Based on total dose, garadacimab Cmax and AUC were concluded to increase in a dose-proportional manner between IV doses of 0.1 and 10 mg/kg. Among SC cohorts, garadacimab AUC was noted to increase dose-proportionally between doses of 1 and 10 mg/kg as the 90% CI for the estimated slopes were contained within the predefined critical interval of 0.7 to 1.3. Note that per Table 71, this is applicable to analyses conducted based on total dose, but not based on body weight-adjusted dose, indicating that garadacimab exposure increases in a
L1093: slightly less than dose-proportional manner. Following an SC dose, C_{max} was found to increase in a slightly less than dose-proportional manner between doses of 1 and 10 mg/kg.

L1108: ## PD Results
L1109: FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity was assessed as a pharmacodynamic (PD) marker. Garadacimab showed dose-dependent inhibition of FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity (Figure 27 and Figure 28). A clear inhibition trend was observed at the highest doses of 3 or 10 mg/kg in both IV and SC cohorts when compared to placebo group. The duration of inhibition was also longer for the higher doses with a return to baseline range at about Day 56 for 3 mg/kg IV or SC, and at about Day 85 for 10 mg/kg IV. The FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity following 10 mg/kg SC was kept slightly lower than baseline at Day 85. In SC cohorts, the time of peak response appears to correlate with the garadacimab Tmax, suggesting a real-time PK/PD relationship.

L1116: ## **Immunogenicity Results**
L1117: No subject in the study developed garadacimab ADAs.
L1118: ## **14.2.3. Study 1003 (SAD Study in Healthy Japanese and Caucasian Subjects)**
L1119: ## **Title**
L1120: A two-part, phase 1, single-center, open-label, single ascending dose study to investigate the PKs, PDs, safety, and tolerability of SC and IV garadacimab in healthy adult Japanese and Caucasian subjects.
L1121: ## **Objectives**
L1122: The primary objectives of this study were to characterize the PK of garadacimab in Japanese subjects after SC dosing and to compare the PK of garadacimab in Japanese and Caucasian subjects at the 200 mg SC dose. Further characterization of PK (including in IV cohorts), PD (FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity), and immunogenicity were the secondary objectives.
L1123: ## **Study Design**
L1124: Study 1003 was designed as a two-part, single-center, open-label, single ascending dose study in healthy adult Japanese and Caucasian subjects (Figure 29). All subjects received a single IV or SC dose of garadacimab and were followed until 85 days postdose. A total of five cohorts were
L1125: enrolled (N=36; n=12/cohort for Cohorts 1a and 1b; n=4 for Cohorts 2 to 4). Parts A and B evaluated SC and IV administration, respectively. Cohort enrollment and dosing was as follows:
L1126: - Cohort 1a: 12 Japanese subjects – 200 mg SC
- Cohort 1b: 12 Caucasian subjects, weight-matched \pm 15% to Cohort 1a – 200 mg SC
- Cohort 2: 4 Japanese subjects – 600 mg SC
- Cohort 3: 4 Japanese subjects – 3 mg/kg IV
- Cohort 4: 4 Japanese subjects – 10 mg/kg IV
L1127: SC doses were administered using a 170 mg/mL solution in 1.2 mL PFS-NSD devices. SC injections were administered as 1\times 1.2 mL (200 mg) or 3\times 1.2 mL (600 mg), with preferred site of injection in the abdomen. IV doses were administered using a 100 mg/mL formulation in a vial presentation. IV doses were given as an infusion at a volume of 60 mL over 60 min.
L1128: The study enrolled healthy male or female subjects aged 18 to 55 years, with body weight between 50 and 100 kg, and body mass index between 18 and &lt;30 kg/m^2, all inclusive. Caucasian subjects were weight-matched \pm 15\% 1:1 with Japanese subjects. Use of prescription or over-the-counter medications, including herbal products and dietary supplements, anticoagulant therapy or drugs with antiplatelet action were prohibited within 21 days before administration of the investigational product. All medications were prohibited during the study except for acetaminophen, and oral or intranasal medications to treat seasonal allergies. Hormonal contraceptives were also permitted.
L1129: **Figure 29. Study Design, Study 1003**
L1130: Source: Figure 9-1, Clinical Study Report for study 1003.
Abbreviations: CSL312, garadacimab; IV, intravenous; SC, subcutaneous
L1131: ## **PK, PD, and Immunogenicity Sampling**
L1132: Blood PK samples were collected at predose on Day 1, and postdose at Hours 0.5 (IV only), 1, 8, 24, 32 (SC only), and 48 and Days 5, 8, 15, 22, 29, 43, 64, and 85.
L1133: Samples to assess FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity were collected at the same time points as PK samples. Immunogenicity samples were collected predose on Day 1, and on Days 29 and 85.
L1134: ## **Subject Disposition and Demographics**
L1135: A total of 37 subjects received treatment, including 12 Japanese subjects in Cohort 1a, 13 Caucasian subjects in Cohort 1b, and 4 subjects each in Cohorts 2 to 4. One Caucasian subject in Cohort 1b was lost to follow-up and discontinued from the study. Overall, 36 subjects completed the study.
L1136: Among the SC cohorts, the mean (SD) body weight in Cohort 1a (Japanese, 200 mg), Cohort 1b (Caucasian, 200 mg), and Cohort 2 (Japanese, 600 mg) were 64.6 (9.6) kg, 63.6 (9.6) kg, and 64.1 (11.4) kg, respectively. Body weight at baseline and garadacimab doses received in IV cohorts are shown in Table 73.

L1142: ## **PK Analysis**
L1143: All 37 subjects were included in the PK analysis set comprised of subjects who received garadacimab and had at least one quantifiable concentration measurement. PK parameters were estimated using noncompartmental analyses.
L1144: During analysis, it was noted that one subject in Cohort 1a, a Japanese subject who received 200 mg SC garadacimab, presented with much higher garadacimab concentrations from 1 to 48 h postdose relative to other subjects (subject [REDACTED](b) (6)). Garadacimab concentrations for this subject began at 69.3 µg/mL at 1 h postdose, peaked at 69.8 µg/mL at 8 h postdose and then declined to 41.2 µg/mL by 48 h postdose (data derived from Listing 16.2.5.2, Appendix 16.2.6, CSR for study 1003). In Cohort 1a, all remaining subjects (11/12, 92%) had garadacimab concentrations below the limit of quantitation at 1 h postdose, and the median concentrations at 8 and 48 h postdose were 2.9 and 7.2 µg/mL, respectively (data derived from Table 14.4.1.1.1, CSR for study 1003).
L1145: The Applicant notes that the reason for these higher values in subject [REDACTED](b) (6) has not been identified. However, an additional analysis was performed in which data from this subject were excluded for comparison.
L1146: ## **PK Results**
L1147: Mean (SD) garadacimab concentration-time profiles by dose group for SC cohorts are shown in **Figure 30**. PK parameters by dose group are shown in **Table 74**. Across SC cohorts, T_{max} was approximately 6 to 7 days, and the mean half-life was approximately 18 to 19 days. These data are consistent with observations in study 1001. Between doses of 200 and 600 mg SC, C_{max} and AUC_{0-inf} increased approximately two-fold for a three-fold increase in dose.

L1176: ## **PD Results**
L1177: FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity was assessed as a PD marker. Garadacimab showed dose-dependent inhibition of FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity (Figure 32 and Figure 33). In SC cohorts, the time of peak response was approximately correlated with the garadacimab T_{max}. Mean inhibition was greater in Japanese relative to Caucasian subjects. However, there was large variability in measured kallikrein activity in both cohorts. Among SC cohorts, the most potent inhibition was observed at the 600 mg dose. The 3 and 10 mg/kg IV cohorts showed inhibition of FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity. Activity returned to baseline by about Day 64 for the 200 mg SC and 3 mg/kg IV cohorts, whereas activity did not return to baseline by Day 85 for the 600 mg SC or 10 mg/kg IV cohorts. In study 1001, a return to baseline in FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity was observed by Day 85 for cohorts receiving 10 mg/kg IV. The differences observed across studies may be due to variability of PD.
L1178: **Figure 32. Mean (SD) FXIIa-Mediated Kallikrein Activity by Dose in SC Cohorts, Study 1003**
L1179: Source: Figure 11-4, Clinical Study Report for study 1003.
Abbreviations: CSL312, garadacimab; SC, subcutaneous

L1183: ## Immunogenicity Results
L1184: No subject in the study developed garadacimab ADAs.
L1185: ## 14.2.4. Trial 2001 (Dose-Ranging Trial in Subjects With HAE)
L1186: ## Title
L1187: A multicenter, randomized, placebo-controlled, parallel-arm trial to investigate the efficacy, PKs, and safety of garadacimab in subjects with hereditary angioedema.
L1188: ## Objectives
L1189: The primary objective was to evaluate the efficacy of garadacimab in the prevention of HAE attacks in subjects with C1-INH HAE. Characterization of PK in subjects with HAE was included as a secondary objective, and evaluation of PD was an exploratory objective.
L1190: ## Trial Design
L1191: Trial 2001 was designed as a multicenter, randomized, placebo-controlled, parallel-arm trial in subjects with HAE (N=50) (Figure 65). Following a 4- to 8-week Run-in Period, subjects entered
L1192: Treatment Period 1, which lasted for approximately 13 weeks. The first 32 subjects with C1-INH HAE (i.e., Type I or II HAE) were randomized 1:1:1:1 to one of the following treatments:
L1193: - Garadacimab: 40 mg IV loading dose, then 75 mg SC Q4W beginning 1 week later.
- Garadacimab: 100 mg IV loading dose, then 200 mg SC Q4W beginning 1 week later.
- Garadacimab: 300 mg IV loading dose, then 600 mg SC Q4W beginning 1 week later.
- Placebo IV, then placebo SC Q4W beginning 1 week later.
L1194: These doses were selected based on a PK/PD model developed to quantify the relationship between garadacimab concentrations and FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity from data in healthy subjects in study 1001. Based on the model, dosages of 75, 200, and 600 mg Q4W were predicted to result in at least 75% of subjects reaching percentage inhibitions of FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity of \ge 30, \ge 50, and \ge 90\%, respectively. IV loading doses were selected to yield exposures similar to those after SC doses based on approximate 50% bioavailability, but with achievement of earlier T_{max}. The Q4W dosing interval was based on an observed 19-day half-life in study 1001.
L1195: After the first 32 subjects with C1-INH HAE were randomized, up to an additional 8 subjects were assigned to an open-label arm in which all subjects received garadacimab at a dosage of 400 mg SC every 2 weeks (Q2W). These subjects did not receive an initial IV loading dose.
L1196: Up to 10 eligible subjects with FXII/plasminogen (PLG) HAE (i.e., Type III HAE with normal C1-esterase inhibitor and genetically confirmed factor XII or PLG gene mutation) were assigned to a separate open-label arm in which all subjects received garadacimab beginning with a 300 mg IV loading dose followed by 600 mg SC Q4W beginning 1 week later.
L1197: All subjects in Treatment Period 1 were eligible for participation in Treatment Period 2, which was conducted in an open-label manner and lasted for at least 44 weeks. Subjects were assigned to treatment as follows:
L1198: - C1-INH HAE subjects who received 75 mg SC Q4W or received open-label 400 mg SC Q2W in Treatment Period 1 were randomized 1:1 to 200 or 600 mg SC Q4W.
- C1-INH HAE subjects who received 200 mg SC Q4W in Treatment Period 1 continued on 200 mg SC Q4W.
- C1-INH HAE subjects who received 600 mg SC Q4W in Treatment Period 1 continued on 600 mg SC Q4W.
- FXII/PLG HAE subjects who received 600 mg SC Q4W in Treatment Period 1 continued on 600 mg SC Q4W.
L1199: All C1-INH HAE subjects who were receiving the 600-mg dose in Treatment Period 2 had their dose reduced to 200 mg SC Q4W at the next scheduled study visit, effective with Protocol Amendment 2.
L1200: IV and SC doses were administered using a 100 mg/mL formulation in a vial presentation. In blinded arms, doses were volume-normalized to maintain the blind. IV doses were given as an infusion at a volume of 3 mL over 3 min. SC doses were administered at a volume of 6 mL in Treatment Period 1, or at a volume of 2 mL or 6 mL for doses of 200 or 600 mg, respectively, in
L1201: Treatment Period 2. The preferred site of injection for SC doses was the abdomen, although another anatomic location could have been used at the discretion of the investigator.
L1202: The study enrolled male or female subjects aged 18 to 65 years with a diagnosis of C1-INH HAE or FXII/PLG HAE.
L1203: Routine prophylaxis to prevent HAE attacks with the use of C1-INH products or antifibrinolytics was prohibited during the Run-in Period and during the Treatment Periods. In addition, the following were prohibited during the 3 months before Screening and at any time during the study: angiotensin-converting enzyme inhibitors, and anticoagulants or antiplatelets.
L1204: Permitted therapies included prescribed medications required for management of acute or chronic medical conditions, and therapies to treat any AEs experienced during the study. Oral contraceptives or hormonal replacement therapy were permitted if taken at a stable dose in the 3 months prior to Screening. On-demand HAE therapy was permitted at any time during the study for the treatment of HAE attacks, including plasma-derived or recombinant C1-INH, icatibant, and ecallantide.
L1205: For additional details on the design of Study 2001, refer to Section 15.3.
L1206: ## **PK, PD, and Immunogenicity Sampling**
L1207: ## **Treatment Period 1**
L1208: ## **C1-INH HAE and FXII/PLG HAE Subjects Receiving Drug Q4W**
L1209: Blood samples to assess PK and PD were collected at predose on Day 1 and Weeks 1, 5, and 9. Postdose samples were collected on Day 1 at Hours 0.5, 4, and 8; and on Days 66 (~72 h after the Week 9 dose), 70, 77, 84, 91, and 186. Note that for subjects who participated in Treatment Period 2, the Day 91 (Week 13) visit was the first visit of Treatment Period 2, and no sample would be collected at the follow-up visit on Day 186.
L1210: Samples for coagulation labs were collected predose on Day 1 and on Days 29, 57, and 85. Immunogenicity samples were collected at predose on Day 1, and on Day 91.
L1211: ## **C1-INH HAE Subjects Receiving Drug Q2W**
L1212: Blood samples to assess PK and PD were collected at predose on Day 1 and Weeks 3, 5, 7, 9, and 11. Postdose samples were collected on Days 64, 77, 85, and 180. Note that for subjects who participated in Treatment Period 2, the Day 85 visit was the first visit of Treatment Period 2, and no sample would be collected at the follow-up visit on Day 180.
L1213: Samples for coagulation labs were collected predose on Day 1 and on Days 35, 63, and 91. Immunogenicity samples were collected at predose on Day 1, and on Day 85.
L1214: ## **Treatment Period 2**
L1215: Blood samples to assess PK and PD were collected at predose on Day 85 or 91 (Week 13, day depends on whether subjects received garadacimab SC Q4W or Q2W), and at Weeks 17, 21, 33, 45, 57, and 70. Samples for coagulation labs were collected at the same time points, with the exception of Week 70.
L1216: Immunogenicity samples were collected at predose on Day 85 or 91, and at Week 57.
L1217: ## **Subject Disposition and Demographics**
L1218: A total of 52 C1-INH subjects entered the Run-in Period and 38 subjects completed the Run-in Period and were assigned to one of the treatment arms as follows:
L1219: - Eight subjects received blinded placebo.
- Nine subjects received blinded garadacimab 75 mg SC Q4W.
- Eight subjects received blinded garadacimab 200 mg SC Q4W.
- Seven subjects received blinded garadacimab 600 mg SC Q4W.
- Six subjects received open-label garadacimab 400 mg SC Q2W.
L1220: All the 38 subjects completed Treatment Period 1 and entered Treatment Period 2. Twenty subjects received 200 mg SC Q4W and eighteen subjects received 600 mg SC Q4W. See Section 16.1 for additional details.
L1221: All the 20 subjects assigned to 200 mg SC Q4W completed Treatment Period 2. Two of these subjects had doses uptitrated to 400 mg Q4W during the study. Note that per protocol, dose increases were allowed at the discretion of the Investigator for subjects who experienced \ge 3 HAE attacks within an 8-week period. A third subject who had originally been assigned to receive 600 mg SC Q4W was downtitrated to 200 mg SC Q4W and later uptitrated to 400 mg SC Q4W. The median (range) time to dose uptitration for all subjects was 142 (63, 167) days.
L1222: Of 18 subjects assigned to 600 mg SC Q4W, 16 completed Treatment Period 2, with two discontinuations prior to downtitration. Of a total of 18 subjects originally assigned to 600 mg SC Q4W, 16 were downtitrated to a dose of 200 mg Q4W, 12 of whom were downtitrated due to Protocol Amendment 2. Of the four subjects who were downtitrated to 200 mg Q4W prior to Protocol Amendment 2, one subject had the dose decreased remotely following remote reconsent before the next scheduled visit. The remaining three subjects had doses reduced as action taken due to AEs as follows:
L1223: - One subject (b) (6) had mild rash.
- One subject (b) (6) had moderate headache and photopsia.
- One subject (b) (6) had mild dyspnea at rest and palpitations.
L1224: A total of seven FXII/PLG HAE subjects entered the Run-in Period and six subjects completed the Run-in Period and were assigned to treatment in Treatment Period 1. The six subjects comprised three subjects with FXII HAE and three subjects with PLG HAE. Of the six subjects, five completed Treatment Period 1, with one discontinuation (FXII HAE subject) due to lack of efficacy. Three subjects, all with PLG HAE, did not respond to treatment and completed the study after Treatment Period 1. The remaining two subjects, both with FXII HAE, were considered responders to therapy. These subjects entered and completed Treatment Period 2.
L1225: Across all treatment groups in Treatment Period 1, the median (interquartile range) body weight was as follows (data derived from Tables 14.1.3.1.1.1 and 14.1.3.1.1.2, CSR for Trial 2001):
L1226: - Placebo (n=8): 80.4 (77.8, 87.5) kg
- 75 mg Q4W (n=9): 96.3 (55.3, 100) kg
- 200 mg Q4W (n=8): 96.5 (81.9, 107) kg
- 600 mg Q4W (n=7): 74.4 (59.0, 85.5) kg
- Open-label 400 mg Q2W (n=6): 85.0 (73.0, 87.0) kg
- FXII/PLG HAE (n=6): 70.7 (56.5, 89.3) kg
L1227: ## Dose Response for Efficacy
L1228: The primary endpoint of the study was the time-normalized number of monthly HAE attacks on treatment with 200 or 600 mg garadacimab compared to placebo in Treatment Period 1. The time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month in subjects with C1-INH HAE by dose cohort in Treatment Period 1 is shown in Table 78. A reduction relative to placebo in the mean time-normalized number of monthly HAE attacks was observed for all dosages evaluated in Treatment Period 1. No additional numerical reduction in the time-normalized number of monthly HAE attacks was observed in the 600 mg Q4W and 400 mg Q2W dosing groups compared to the 200 mg Q4W dosing group. See Section 16.1 for additional details. This trend continued into Treatment Period 2 in which the mean (SD) time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month was 0.08 (0.18) and 0.22 (0.35) for subjects receiving 200 mg Q4W and 600 mg Q4W, respectively (data derived from Table 11-6, CSR for Trial 2001). Data from Treatment Period 2 should be interpreted with caution as it was conducted in an open-label manner.

L1234: ## **PK Analysis**
L1235: The 38 C1-INH subjects who received garadacimab in Treatment Periods 1 and 2 were included in the PK population, which comprised all subjects for whom at least one measurable concentration of garadacimab was reported. All the six FXII/PLG HAE subjects were included in the PK population in Treatment Period 1. Both FXII/PLG HAE subjects were included in the PK population in Treatment Period 2.
L1236: ## PK Results
L1237: ## Treatment Period 1
L1238: Mean (SD) garadacimab concentration-time profiles by dose group for blinded cohorts (i.e., 75, 200, or 600 mg Q4W with IV loading doses of 40, 100, and 300 mg, respectively) are shown in Figure 34, for the open-label C1-INH cohort (i.e., 400 mg Q2W with no IV loading dose) in Figure 35, and for the FXII/PLG HAE cohort (300 mg IV then 600 mg Q4W) in Figure 36. For all cohorts that received an IV loading dose, peak concentrations were reached after the end of IV infusion. Following the beginning of SC dosing, plasma concentrations declined, with steady state reached after three doses (approximately Day 35, Week 5). In subjects receiving 400 mg SC Q2W with no IV loading dose, steady state appears to have been reached by Day 56 (Week 8).

L1257: A summary of steady state PK parameters following SC dosing in blinded treatment arms (C1-INH HAE subjects) and in subjects with FXII/PLG HAE is shown in Table 79. Across all treatment groups, median T_{max} was approximately 6 to 7 days postdose, and the mean half-life ranged from 14.5 to 18.5 days. These values are consistent with those reported for healthy subjects following a single SC dose in studies 1001, 1003, and 1004. C_{max} and area under the concentration-time curve during the dosing interval appeared to increase in a slightly less-than-dose proportional manner between doses of 75 and 600 mg, with 5.3-fold and 5.9-fold increases, respectively, for an 8-fold increase in dose. There was no apparent difference in garadacimab exposure between subjects with C1-INH HAE and subjects with FXII/PLG HAE.

L1269: ## **PD Results**
L1270: FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity was assessed as a PD marker. In the blinded treatment arms, garadacimab showed dose-dependent inhibition of FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity (Table 80). Following administration of the IV loading dose, inhibition of FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity was observed within 8 h postdose. Inhibition at all doses was maintained at trough throughout the Treatment Period, with the lowest activity observed among subjects receiving 600 mg Q4W. Changes in FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity were comparable between C1-INH and FXII/PLG HAE subjects who received 300 mg IV then 600 mg SC Q4W.

L1275: All treatment arms with C1-INH HAE subjects were conducted in a blinded manner. The treatment arm with FXII/PLG HAE subjects was conducted in an open-label manner.
L1276: Abbreviations: A, absorbance; C1-INH, C1-esterase inhibitor; FXII, Factor XII; PLG, plasminogen; Q4W, every 4 weeks
L1277: For C1-INH HAE subjects who received 400 mg SC Q2W without an IV loading dose, mean (SD) FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity decreased from 0.20 (0.05) on Day 1 (predose) to 0.09 (0.07) at Week 3, 0.07 (0.03) at Week 9, 0.04 (0.05) at Week 10, 0.08 (0.03) at Week 11, 0.04 (0.04) at Week 12, and 0.06 (0.04) at Week 13 (data derived from Table 14.2.3.3.2.2, CSR for Trial 2001). Decreases in activity were observed after the first dose and maintained throughout the Treatment Period. Note that doses were dispensed on Day 1 and Weeks 3, 5, 7, 9, and 11. Thus, measurements at Weeks 10 and 12 occurred postdose. The lower mean activity at those time points may thus be attributed to higher plasma garadacimab concentrations.
L1278: Dose-dependent inhibition in FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity was maintained in Treatment Period 2. From Week 17 onwards, mean (SD) FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity ranged from 0.14 (0.04) to 0.17 (0.06) for the 200 mg SC Q4W arm, and from 0.8 (0.05) to 0.10 (0.05) for the 600 mg SC Q4W arm (data derived from Table 14.2.3.3.2.3, CSR for Trial 2001). Values observed for the 200 mg SC Q4W arm were similar to baseline levels for all treatment arms.
L1279: ## Activated Partial Thromboplastin Time
L1280: Per the Applicant, the normal range for aPTT values is between 24.3 and 43.4 seconds. During Treatment Period 1, in the 75 and 200 mg Q4W treatment arms, mean aPTT values post-first dose fell within this range at all time points (mean [SD] values range from 24.88 [3.23] to 28.26 [3.01] seconds). In these cohorts, maximum values did not exceed the upper limit of normal. No difference from placebo was apparent.
L1281: In the 600 mg Q4W cohort, mean post-first dose values were in general higher than those in the 75 and 200 mg Q4W cohorts (mean [SD] values range from 42.02 [17.8] to 47.95 [29.1] seconds). In the 600 mg Q4W treatment arms, the mean aPTT was above normal range on Days 35 and 63 with a mean [SD] value of 47.50 [31.69] seconds. Although mean values did not exceed the upper limit of normal on Day 91, maximum values indicate that at least some subjects exceeded the normal range. Per the Applicant, this prolongation in Treatment Period 1 was observed in three subjects (data derived from Table 14.2.3.3.7.1, CSR for Trial 2001).
L1282: In the 400 mg Q2W treatment arm, the mean aPTT was within normal range at all time points. Maximum values fell within the normal range at all time points except for the Day 85 time point
L1283: (mean [SD] aPTT at this time point was 36.38 [11.65] seconds) (data derived from Table 14.2.3.3.7.2, CSR for Trial 2001).
L1284: In Treatment Period 2, the mean aPTT was within the normal range at all time points for the 200 mg Q4W cohort (mean [SD] values range from 26.85 [4.22] to 28.68 [6.62] seconds). Maximum values exceeded normal range at Week 57. In the 600 mg Q4W cohort, mean values were in general higher than those in the 200 mg Q4W cohort (mean [SD] values range from 31.33 [10.7] to 44.3 [34.6] seconds). Maximum values exceeded normal range at all time points (data derived from Table 14.2.3.3.7.3, CSR for Trial 2001).
L1285: ## **Immunogenicity Results**
L1286: No subject treated with garadacimab tested positive for ADAs during Treatment Period 1. One subject randomized to placebo tested positive for ADAs at week 13 (i.e., the end of Treatment Period 1). This subject received 200 mg Q4W garadacimab in Treatment Period 2. No ADAs were detected in subsequent visits.
L1287: Note that immunogenicity samples were collected on Day 1, Week 13, and Week 57. Due to the sampling scheme, subjects with transient ADAs may not have been captured. Regardless, the data suggest that subjects likely did not have persistent ADAs.
L1288: Also note that the drug tolerance of the ADA assay is adequate up to 30 µg/mL garadacimab (refer to Office of Pharmaceutical Quality executive summary review dated August 26, 2024). At ADA sample collection time points, there were six subjects with seven samples with garadacimab concentrations exceeding 30 µg/mL, including three subjects who received 400 mg Q2W (concentrations of 46.0 to 54.1 µg/mL at Day 85) and three subjects who received 600 mg Q4W (one with a concentration of 31.4 µg/mL at Day 91, one with a concentration of 43.7 µg/mL at Day 91 and 36.5 µg/mL at Week 57, and one with a concentration of 32.0 µg/mL at Week 57).
L1289: ## **14.2.5. Trial 3001 (Phase 3 Efficacy Trial in Subjects With Type I and II HAE)**
L1290: ## **Title**
L1291: A multicenter, double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled, parallel-arm trial to investigate the efficacy and safety of SC administration of garadacimab in the prophylactic treatment of HAE.
L1292: ## **Study Design**
L1293: For a detailed description of the design of trial 3001, refer to Section 6.2.1.
L1294: Trial 3001 was designed as a multicenter, double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled, parallel-arm trial in adolescent and adult subjects with HAE. Following a Run-in Period of up to 2 months, subjects were randomized 3:2 to receive either garadacimab or placebo during the 6-month Treatment Period. Subjects on garadacimab received a 400 mg SC loading dose (administered as 2×200 mg) followed by 200 mg SC once monthly beginning on Day 31. Randomization was stratified by age (≤17 years or &gt;17 years).
L1295: SC doses were administered using a 170 mg/mL solution in 1.2 mL PFS-NSD devices. SC injections were administered as 1×1.2 mL (200 mg) or 2×1.2 mL (400 mg) in the abdomen.
L1296: The 200-mg dose was selected based on efficacy, safety, PK, and PD observed in Treatment Period 1 of trial 2001, with support from exposure-response (E-R) modeling. An E-R model was constructed to simulate HAE attack rates over various garadacimab concentrations. The median predicted minimum daily average garadacimab concentrations at steady state following a 200 mg SC once monthly regimen corresponded to 90% relative risk reduction in HAE attack rate. Increasing the dose beyond 200 mg was not predicted to result in further reduction in HAE attack rate.
L1297: In end-of-Phase Two Meeting Minutes dated October 17, 2020 in DARRTS under IND 139936 (reference ID: 4687429), the Agency noted that the proposed dose and dose interval are supported by the phase 2 dose-ranging trial (2001), but noted that a different formulation from that used in 2001 will be used. As a result, the choice of the proposed dose and dosing interval was left at the risk and discretion of the Applicant. In addition, the Agency also requested that the Applicant provide justification for use of a loading dose.
L1298: The trial enrolled male or female subjects aged \ge 12 years with a diagnosis of clinically confirmed C1-INH HAE (i.e., Type I or II HAE). No subject with FXII/PLG HAE diagnosis (i.e., HAE with normal C1-esterase inhibitor and genetically confirmed factor XII or PLG gene mutation) was enrolled in this trial.
L1299: For adults, use of C1-INH products, androgens, antifibrinolytics, or other small-molecule medications for routine prophylaxis against HAE within 2 weeks prior to the Run-in Period was exclusionary. For adolescents aged 12 to 17 years, use of long-term prophylactic therapy for HAE before screening was exclusion. Subjects were also excluded if they used lanadelumab within 3 months prior to the Run-in Period, or use of systemically absorbed estrogen-containing medications (e.g., oral contraceptives or hormone replacement therapy) or angiotensin-converting enzyme inhibitors within 4 weeks prior to the Run-in Period.
L1300: Permitted therapies included prescribed medications required for management of acute or chronic medical conditions, and therapies to treat any AEs experienced during the trial. On-demand HAE therapy was permitted at any time during the trial for the treatment of HAE attacks, including plasma-derived or recombinant C1-INH, icatibant, and ecallantide.
L1301: ## **PK, PD, and Immunogenicity Sampling**
L1302: Blood samples to assess PK and PD (i.e., FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity) were collected on Days 1, 31, 62, 91, 121, 151, 182 (Month 7, end of the Treatment Period), and 242 (final follow-up visit).
L1303: Samples for coagulation labs were collected at Screening, predose on Day 1, and on Days 91 and 182 (Month 7, end of the Treatment Period).
L1304: Immunogenicity samples were collected on Day 1, and on Days 91 and 182.
L1305: ## **Subject Disposition and Demographics**
L1306: For details, see Section 6.2.4. A total of 76 subjects entered the Run-in Period and 65 subjects completed the Run-in Period and were randomized to treatment, including 39 who received garadacimab and 26 subjects who received placebo. One subject in the placebo arm was randomized to blinded treatment in error and never received study treatment. Of the remaining 64 subjects, 61 completed treatment and 60 completed the study. Three subjects, all in the
L1307: placebo group, discontinued treatment due to withdrawal. Of 60 subjects who completed the study, 57 entered open-label extension study 3002, comprising 36 subjects who received garadacimab and 21 subjects who received placebo.
L1308: Among adult subjects randomized to garadacimab, the median (range) body weight at Screening was 79 (58.4, 140) kg. The study enrolled six adolescent subjects ranging in age from 12 to 17 years. Four adolescents received garadacimab and two received placebo. Among adolescent subjects receiving garadacimab, the median (range) age was 15 (12, 17) years, and the median (range) body weight at Screening was 60.3 (54.3, 79) kg.
L1309: ## **PK Analysis**
L1310: All the 39 subjects who received garadacimab were included in the PK population, which comprised all the subjects with at least one measurable concentration of garadacimab.
L1311: Plasma concentrations were summarized by nominal time points and plotted on linear and semilogarithmic scales. Noncompartmental analysis was not conducted. PK data were included in analyses of population PK, PK/PD, and E-R.
L1312: ## **PK Results**
L1313: The mean (SD) garadacimab concentration-time profile for all subjects is shown in Figure 38. Following administration of the SC loading dose, the highest trough concentrations were observed on Day 30 prior to the second dose (mean [SD]=10,533 [4004] ng/mL). Plasma concentrations declined by Day 60 (prior to administration of the third dose) and remained consistent for the remainder of the Treatment Period. Mean (SD) trough concentrations from Day 60 through the remainder of the Treatment Period ranged from 8087 (4277) ng/mL to 8692 (3934) ng/mL. Steady-state for C_{\text{trough}} was reached by Day 60. This behavior is similar to observations in trial 2001, in which subjects received an IV loading dose prior to initiation of SC dosing.
L1314: **Figure 38. Mean (SD) Garadacimab Concentration-Time Profile in All Subjects, Trial 3001**
L1315: Source: Figure 14.2.5.2.1, Clinical Study Report for trial 3001.
Abbreviation: CSL312, garadacimab
L1316: The mean (SD) garadacimab concentration-time profiles for adults versus adolescents are shown in Figure 39. The PK of garadacimab in adolescents appears consistent with that in adults. The mean (SD) garadacimab trough concentrations in adolescents from Day 60 through the remainder of the Treatment Period ranged from 7297 (2085) ng/mL to 9688 (2421) ng/mL. In adults, trough concentrations ranged from 7969 (4257) ng/mL to 8593 (4006) ng/mL (data derived from Table 14.2.5.1.2, CSR for trial 3001).
L1317: **Figure 39. Mean (SD) Garadacimab Concentration-Time Profiles in Adults Versus Adolescents, Trial 3001**
L1318: Source: Figure 14.2.5.2.3, Clinical Study Report for trial 3001.
Abbreviation: CSL312, garadacimab
L1319: The final pooled population PK model was used to simulate steady state PK parameters for all subjects in trial 3001. Refer to Section 14.5 for additional details on the population PK analysis. Parameter estimates are shown in Table 81. Exposure following the initial 400 mg SC loading dose was also predicted using the final population PK model. The mean (SD) model predicted AUC_{\tau} and C_{\max} following the 400 mg SC loading dose were 13700 (4140) ng\cdoth/mL and 28.5 (9.0) \mug/mL. Garadacimab exposure after the loading dose is thus about 133% of that at steady state following administration of 200 mg SC once monthly (refer to Applicant's response to IR, submitted September 20, 2024).

L1327: FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity was assessed as a PD marker. At predose time points, approximately 20% inhibition of FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity was observed.
