L1334: ## **Activated Partial Thromboplastin Time**
L1335: Per the Applicant, the normal range for aPTT values is between 24.3 and 43.4 seconds. For subjects in the garadacimab arm, there was little change in mean aPTT values throughout the Treatment Period. Mean (SD) aPTT at baseline, Day 91, and Day 182 were 29.26 (9.07) seconds, 28.84 (5.59) seconds, and 33.36 (19.8) seconds, respectively (data derived from Table 14.3.4.4, CSR for trial 3001). There were three subjects noted to have aPTT prolongation following administration of garadacimab:
L1336: - One adult subject with aPTT values of 59.4 and 95.5 seconds on Days 91 and 182, respectively.
- One adolescent subject (aged 17 years) with an aPTT of 67 seconds on Day 182, and
- One adult subject with an aPTT of 126 seconds on Day 182.
L1337: The C_{\text{trough}} values from these three subjects at the four time points listed above were not outliers or consistently at the higher end of the distribution of population C_{\text{trough}} values observed from trial 3001 (see Section 7.7.1).
L1338: ## **Immunogenicity Results**
L1339: Two subjects in the garadacimab arm tested positive for ADAs, including one subject at baseline (prior to receiving garadacimab) and one subject on Day 182, both with a titer value of 10. The subject who tested positive at baseline did not have detectable antibodies at subsequent visits.
L1340: ## 14.2.6. Study 3002 (Long-Term Open-Label Extension Study in Subjects With HAE)
L1341: ## Title
L1342: An open-label study to evaluate the long-term safety and efficacy of garadacimab in the prophylactic treatment of hereditary angioedema.
L1343: ## Study Design
L1344: For a detailed description of the study design of 3002, refer to Sections 7.6.2 and 15.2.
L1345: Study 3002 is ongoing. An interim CSR was provided with a data cutoff of February 13, 2023 for all subjects, and May 22, 2023 for adolescent subjects (i.e., when 8/10 adolescents had \ge 12 months of exposure).
L1346: Study 3002 was designed as a multicenter, open-label, phase 3b study in adolescent and adult subjects with HAE. This study enrolled rollover subjects with HAE from studies 2001 and 3001, as well as newly enrolled subjects with Type I or Type II HAE. All subjects received garadacimab. Rollover subjects continued to receive garadacimab at a dosage of 200 mg SC once monthly. Following a Run-in Period, garadacimab-naïve subjects received a 400 mg SC loading dose (2\times 200 mg) followed by 200 mg SC once monthly beginning one month later. Each subject was to receive garadacimab for at least 12 months.
L1347: SC doses were administered using a 170 mg/mL solution in 1.2 mL prefilled syringe or AI devices. SC injections were administered as 1\times 1.2 mL (200 mg) or 2\times 1.2 mL (400 mg) in the abdomen.
L1348: The study enrolled male or female subjects aged \ge 12 years with a diagnosis of clinically confirmed C1-INH HAE (i.e., Type I or II HAE). Criteria regarding washout of prior medications and use of concomitant medications is similar to that from trial 3001.
L1349: ## PK, PD, and Immunogenicity Sampling
L1350: For subjects from 3001 and all naïve subjects, blood samples to assess PK and PD (i.e., FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity) were collected on Days 1, 31, 61, and 91; Months 6, 9, 12, 18, 24, and 30; and at the end-of-treatment visit.
L1351: For subjects from 2001, blood samples to assess PK and PD were collected on Days 1 and 91; Months 6, 12, 18, 24, and 30; and at the end-of-treatment visit.
L1352: Additional PK/PD samples were collected in a subset of garadacimab-naïve subjects to characterize the PK of the loading dose. Following administration of the loading dose on Day 1, samples were collected on Days 7, 14, 21, and 30.
L1353: For adolescent subjects, additional PK/PD samples were collected following the Month 3 (Day 91) dose on Days 3, 7, 14, 21, and 30 (predose prior to the next dose).
L1354: Samples for coagulation labs were collected at Screening, predose on Day 1, and on Days 91, 181, 361 (Month 12), 541 (Month 18), 721 (Month 24), 901 (Month 30), and at the end-of-treatment visit. Rollover subjects from 2001 did not have a sample collected on Day 91.
L1355: For all subjects, immunogenicity samples were collected on Day 1, and at Months 6, 12, and 24 and at the end-of-treatment visit.
L1356: ## **Subject Disposition and Demographics**
L1357: For details, see Section 7.6.2. A total of 92 subjects rolled over from trials 2001 and 3001. From 2001, 35 subjects were enrolled, including 2 subjects with FXII HAE diagnosis (normal C1-INH with FXII gene mutation). From 3001, 57 subjects were enrolled, including 21 subjects who had received placebo and were therefore considered garadacimab-naïve. A total of 77 subjects with Type I or II HAE who were not previously enrolled in 2001 or 3001 entered the Run-in Period, and 69 subjects entered the Treatment Period.
L1358: One hundred sixty-one subjects entered the Treatment Period, including 90 who were garadacimab-naïve and 71 who were previously treated. Of these, 147 subjects are ongoing. Three subjects completed the study, and 11 prematurely discontinued. One hundred nineteen of the one hundred sixty-one subjects had at least 12 months of exposure to garadacimab.
L1359: Among all subjects, the median (range) body weight at baseline was 78.3 (45.0, 131) kg. The study enrolled 10 adolescents ranging in age from 13 to 17 years. Five adolescents rolled over from trial 3001 (n=3 received garadacimab and n=2 received placebo), and the remaining 5 were newly enrolled garadacimab-naïve subjects. Among adolescents, the median (range) age was 14.5 (13, 17) years, and the median (range) body weight at baseline was 55.7 (45.2, 88) kg.
L1360: ## **PK Analysis**
L1361: All 161 subjects who received garadacimab were included in the PK population, which was comprised of all subjects with at least one measurable concentration of garadacimab.
L1362: Plasma concentrations were summarized by nominal time points and plotted. PK parameters were derived for the PK subgroup of garadacimab-naïve subjects who had additional samples taken following the loading dose (n=15). These included T_{max}, C_{max}, and AUC_{0-30}.
L1363: Noncompartmental analysis was not conducted. PK data were included in analyses of population PK, PK/PD, and E-R.
L1364: ## **PK Results**
L1365: The mean (SD) garadacimab concentration-time profile for all subjects up to the time of data cutoff is shown in Figure 42. At the time of data cutoff, PK data up to 12 months were available for 101 subjects. Most subjects (n=140) had data up to 6 months. From Month 3 through Month 12, mean (SD) steady-state trough concentrations ranged from 7429 (4759) ng/mL to 7882 (4276) ng/mL. Note that data shown include trough concentration data for all subjects (i.e., rollover and garadacimab-naïve subjects). Higher plasma concentrations with greater variability are observed at Month 1 and Month 2 due to some subjects receiving the loading dose.

L1368: Additional PK samples were collected following administration of the 400 mg SC loading dose in a subgroup of garadacimab-naïve subjects (n=15). Peak concentrations were achieved on Day 7 postdose. The mean (SD) C_{max} was 24.5 (11.0) \mug/mL, and the mean (SD) AUC_{0-30} was 10685 (5064) \mug\cdoth/mL. These values are comparable to model-predicted steady state PK parameters from trial 3001 (mean [SD] C_{max}=21.2 [6.6] \mug/mL; mean [SD] AUC_{\tau_{ss}}=10,300 (3380) \mug\cdoth/mL). Refer to Table 81.
L1369: The mean (SD) garadacimab concentration-time profiles for adults (N=151) versus adolescents (N=10) are shown in Figure 43. Garadacimab trough concentrations in adolescents were greater than those in adults, possibly due to lower body weight in adolescent subjects. The mean (SD) garadacimab trough concentrations from Month 3 through Month 12 are summarized in Table 82. From Month 3 through Month 12, garadacimab trough concentrations were 1.2- to 1.8-fold greater in adolescents than in adults. Exposure was 1.8-fold greater at Month 12, a time point at which only three adolescent subjects provided data. Data at Month 12 should therefore be interpreted with caution.

L1378: points, little to no inhibition of FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity relative to baseline was observed.

L1386: This trend of concentration-dependent inhibition of FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity was corroborated by data in adolescent subjects who had additional samples collected following the Month 3 dose. At the time of peak concentration on Days 3 to 7 following the Month 3 dose (administered on Day 90), inhibition to about 50% activity was observed. Throughout the Treatment Period, activity appeared similar between adults and adolescents.

L1389: ## **Activated Partial Thromboplastin Time**
L1390: Per the Applicant, the normal range for aPTT values is between 24.3 and 43.4 seconds. Mean (SD) aPTT at baseline was 29.46 (11.1) seconds. From Month 3 through Month 12, mean (SD) aPTT ranged from 28.55 (3.76) to 30.73 (11.3) seconds. There were 21/161 subjects (13.0%) with 25 cases of aPTT prolongation during the study, including 5 cases at Screening, 5 cases at Day 1, 6 cases at Month 3, 8 cases at Month 6, and 1 case at Month 12. Values above normal range ranged between 45.1 and 126.0 seconds. Most cases were transient (i.e., occurring at a single time point), except in three subjects who had prolongation at multiple time points, including before the first dose of study treatment. Prolongation of aPTT was observed in three adolescent subjects (data derived from Listing 16.2.8.5.1, CSR for study 3002). In two cases, prolongation was observed at Screening only. In the remaining case, prolongation was observed transiently at Month 6.
L1391: - One adolescent subject (b) (6), aged 13 years) had aPTT above normal range at Screening only (61.2 seconds). On Day 1 and Month 6, this subject had an aPTT below the normal range.
- One adolescent subject (b) (6), aged 14 years) had aPTT above normal range at Month 6 (55.2 seconds). The aPTT value was within the normal range at Month 12.
- One adolescent subject (b) (6), aged 15 years) had aPTT above the normal range at Screening only (101.6 seconds). Values were within the normal range at all other time points (Day 1, Month 3, and Month 6).
L1392: ## **Immunogenicity Results**
L1393: Five subjects tested positive for ADAs, including the following:
L1394: - One rollover subject from 3001. This subject tested positive on Day 182 (end of 3001 and baseline of 3002) with a titer value of 10. This subject also tested positive at Month 6 with a titer value of 160.
- Three subjects tested positive at Month 12, each with a titer value of 10.
- One subject tested positive at the End-of-Treatment visit after 5 months of treatment with a titer value of 320.
L1395: ## **14.3. Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance**
L1396: ## **14.3.1. PK Bioanalytical Methods**
L1397: Overall, bioanalytical methods to quantitate garadacimab concentrations in clinical study samples were determined to be acceptable.
L1398: Garadacimab concentrations were determined using an enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay. Briefly, an anti-idiotype antibody specific to garadacimab was coated on a microtiter plate. Following addition of plasma samples, garadacimab is bound to the anti-idiotype antibody. Bound garadacimab is detected using a commercial antihuman lambda chain specific IgG antibody conjugated with horseradish peroxidase. Application of an appropriate substrate produces a blue color in proportion to the number of complexes formed. The bioanalytical method was performed in two laboratories:
L1399: - CSL Research and Clinical BioanalyticsStudies 1001 and 1003, and trial 2001
- Procedure RnD-SOP-000053053 (manual method) was used in 1001, 1003, and 2001
- Procedure RnD-SOP-000053165 (automated method) was used in 1001. Partial validation was conducted to support use of an automated Tecan EVO200 platform. Assay principle and format is unchanged from the manual method
- The method was revalidated for use in 1003 and 2001 due to exhaustion of stocks and standards (reported in RnD-VAL-000141302)
- (b) (4)Study 1004, trial 3001, and study 3002
- Procedure TP80240 was used in 1004 and 3002
- Procedure TP80003 was used in 3001
L1400: Because subjects from trial 2001 rolled into study 3002, concordance testing was performed to compare bioanalytical method performance across the two laboratories.
L1401: Note that for the assay used for 1001 (CSL Research), the reported assay range is 1.25 to 100 ng/mL in 1% human plasma based on 100-fold dilution (i.e., 125 to 10,000 ng/mL in PK samples prior to dilution). For the assay used for 1003 and 2001 (CSL Research) following
L1402: revalidation, the assay range is 1 to 100 ng/mL in 1% human plasma (100 to 10,000 ng/mL in PK samples prior to dilution).
L1403: The assay range for the method used in 1004 and 3002 (TP80240) ranged from 1.01 to 111 ng/mL in 1% human plasma (i.e., 101 to 11100 ng/mL in PK samples prior to dilution). The assay range for the method used in 3001 (TP80003) ranged from 10.6 to 111 ng/mL in 1% human plasma (1060 to 11100 ng/mL in PK samples prior to dilution). The validation report for TP80003 notes that initial evaluations of selectivity at a concentration of 1.01 ng/mL did not meet acceptance criteria. Thus, the lower limit of quantification for TP80003 was increased to 10.6 ng/mL, a concentration at which selectivity evaluations were acceptable.
L1404: Assay procedures TP80240 and TP80003 are very similar, and the key differences include extension of the standard curve in TP80240 to accommodate a wider analytical range, and assessment of the lower limit of quantification. The Applicant conducted a full revalidation to support the wider analytical range. Refer to the Applicant's Information Request Response dated February 20, 2024.

L1441: - Selectivity was assessed in plasma from subjects with HAE (N=20 lots). Individual lots were spiked with garadacimab at concentrations of 1, 5, and 80 ng/mL. Note that 10 lots were spiked at 5 ng/mL, while 10 separate lots (N=20 total) were spiked at 1 and 80 ng/mL. At all concentrations, the %RE and %CV were &lt;20% for at least 8/10 lots.

- Long-term stability in plasma from subjects with HAE is ongoing. Samples are spiked with garadacimab at concentrations of 15,000 and 7,500 ng/mL. Per the Applicant, at the time of response (i.e., February 20, 2024), 10 months stability assessment has been completed and shown to pass acceptance criteria.
L1442: Concordance testing was performed using trial samples from trial 2001 in support of study 3002 as subjects from 2001 were permitted to rollover into 3002. Samples were initially measured using assay procedure RnD-SOP-000053053 (CSL Research) and then measured using assay procedure TP80240 (b) (4). Of 35 samples, 31 (88.6%) fell within 30% of the mean value. The results of concordance testing support the comparability of the method across both laboratories.

L1461: **Table 91. Bioanalytical Method Performance in Study 1001**
L1462: Assay passing rate	136/138 runs met the method acceptance criteria
Two runs were rejected due to curve failure
Yes
Standard curve performance	Cumulative accuracy (% bias) range: -6.1 to 6.0%
Cumulative precision (% CV): \le 8.8\%
Standard curves were generated from 9 standards with concentrations ranging from 1.0 to 111 ng/mL. For 7 out of 9 standards, a minimum of duplicate wells must have a calculated concentration with a CV of \le 20\%. The measured concentration for 7 of the 9 standards must be within 20% of theoretical concentration. The curve fit measured (R-value) must be \ge 0.985.
Note that the LLOQ for the method used in 1001 was 1.25 ng/mL. However standard curve samples went as low as 1.01 ng/mL. In most runs, standard curve samples at 1.01 ng/mL were either below the limit of quantitation or had unacceptable %bias. Thus, in most runs, the LLOQ could be considered the next standard of 1.81 ng/mL. In other runs (n = 5), standards at 1.81 ng/mL also had unacceptable %bias and thus the LLOQ in these runs was 3.3 ng/mL.
Note that study 1001 was a single-dose study. The change in LLOQ is primarily expected to have affected measurement of samples with lower concentrations (i.e., samples at the very beginning and end of time course).
Yes
L1463: QC performance	
Assay acceptance criteria is based on the following: a minimum of duplicate wells of at least 2 of the 3 controls must have a CV of \le 20\%. The measured concentration must be within 20% of the theoretical (expected) concentration for 2 of the 3 controls. The Applicant indicates that 2 out of 3 QCs per level need to be included in the calculation of nominal value (within 20% of nominal).
Cumulative accuracy (% bias) range: 2.3 to 5.1%
Cumulative precision (% CV): \le 9.2\%
Percent total error (%TE): \le 14.1\%
Note that in eight passing runs, the determined mean concentration for the low QC fell outside of acceptance criteria. In one passing run, the determined mean concentration for the medium QC fell outside of acceptance criteria. In five passing runs, the determined mean concentration for the high QC fell outside of acceptance criteria. As replicate data were not provided, accuracy and precision of these runs cannot be guaranteed (i.e., it is unclear whether at least 50% of QCs per level fell within \pm 20\% of nominal values).
Approximately 10% of the passing runs are impacted. There may thus be an impact on the study data reported. Results from study 1001 should be interpreted with caution.

L1479: ## 14.3.2. PD Bioanalytical Methods
L1480: ## 14.3.2.1. Activated Factor XII-Mediated Kallikrein Activity
L1481: To determine activated FXII-mediated kallikrein activity in clinical study samples, a chromogenic enzymatic assay was used to track inhibitory effects of garadacimab on the kallikrein-kinin system. Plasma samples are diluted in a reaction buffer containing dextran sulfate, an activator of the contact system. The chromogenic substrate (S2302), which is enzymatically cleaved by kallikrein and FXIIa, was used to monitor the amidolytic activity generated in plasma samples upon activation of the contact system. Substrate cleavage is monitored at an absorbance of 405 nm (A_{405}) with the change of absorbance per minute as the assay readout. Control plasma samples in the presence and absence of garadacimab or kallikrein were included to approximate clinical samples possessing high, medium, and low levels of amidolytic activity. Note that this assay did not use a calibration standard and thus the accuracy of the method was not determined. Amidolytic activity generated after ex vivo contact activation was reported as a continuous variable to track the level of inhibition by garadacimab. This means that the individual values themselves are not likely to be meaningful.
L1482: Note that there are limitations to the PD assay used to measure FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity. First, the chromogenic substrate S2302 is not specifically cleaved by FXIIa, but rather is also cleaved by kallikrein. Although, presumably, inhibition of FXIIa should also inhibit downstream production of kallikrein. The second is that the method is semiquantitative, as noted above. Thus, individual values determined from the assay are not likely to be meaningful.
L1483: For phase 3 studies, validation was updated for phase 3 drug product and to incorporate frozen controls rather than controls prepared “in-assay.” Robustness was also assessed for use of standard cryovials compared to FluidX tubes.
L1484: - The same assay procedure RnD-SOP-000053087 was used for trials 2001 and 3001, and study 3002. All assays were performed by CSL Research and Clinical BioanalyticsValidation report RnD-VAL-000148888 describes validation of the method in 2001
- Validation report REP-019128 describes validation of the method in 3001 and 3002.
L1485: The range of the assay was determined by spiking kallikrein into prekallikrein-deficient plasma (Table 94 and Table 95). For both validation reports, activity was found to increase at kallikrein concentrations up to 10 U/mL (RnD-VAL-000148888) or 5 U/mL (REP-019128). The Applicant hypothesizes that at higher kallikrein concentrations, amidolytic activity is so rapid that the majority of substrate cleavage occurs prior to initiation of the first absorbance time point of the kinetic read. Thus, the upper limits of the assay are defined at the maximum activity observed for each validation. The assay range is 0.003 to 0.371 \Delta A_{405nm/min} for RnD-VAL-000148888 and 0.005 to 0.298 \Delta A_{405nm/min} for REP-019128. In both reports, FXII-mediated kallikrein activity was measured in the plasma of subjects with HAE (n=6). In all cases, the mean activity fell within the assay range.

L1500: ## **In-Study Bioanalysis**
L1501: FXII-mediated kallikrein activity is most relevant for studies in subjects with HAE. Thus, trials 2001 and 3001 and study 3002 were evaluated.
L1502: - Trial 2001 – REP-019486
- Trial 3001 – REP-020153
- Study 3002 – REP-020155
L1503: The following were observed:
L1504: - Trial 2001 – the %CV for QCs ranged from 12.3 to 26.7%In three runs, the mean activity for the medium control was marked as being “unusually high” meaning values were &gt; 2-fold of the average. The %CV for these samples was \le9.8%
- Trial 3001 – the %CV for QCs ranged from 22.5 to 29.0%
L1505: - Study 3002 – the %CV for QCs ranged from 21.3 to 48.0%%CV for low controls was 39.6%. Across runs, assay values ranged from 0.004 to 0.015, a difference of 0.011. The high %CV observed is likely due to the low values which have a higher sensitivity to smaller changes in measured values.
- %CV for maximum controls (samples spiked with 10 U/mL kallikrein) was 48.0% with assay values ranging from 0.020 to 0.281.
L1506: In report REP-020155 (in-study report for 3002), HAE sample stability was assessed with incurred sample analysis using stored samples from trials 2001 (n=31) and 3001 (n=57). Samples for 2001 were stored for 13 to 18 months, while samples for 3002 were stored for 6 to 13 months. A total of 62.5% (55/88) of retested samples were within 30% difference of the original value, including 21/31 samples (67.7%) from 2001 and 34/57 samples (59.6%) from 3001. The samples from 2001 were stored for a longer period of time than those from 3001 and the number of samples with a percent difference &lt;30% was higher in 2001. This suggests that the method is prone to variability in measurements and the differences observed may not be due to stability issues.
L1507: ## 14.4. Immunogenicity Assessment—Impact of PK/PD, Efficacy, and Safety
L1508: Across clinical studies conducted in subjects with HAE who received garadacimab (n=173), a total of 5 subjects (2.9%) tested positive for treatment-emergent antidrug antibodies (TE-ADAs). Two subjects tested positive prior to receiving garadacimab treatment. These subjects exhibited low ADA titers (i.e., 10). No adolescent subject tested positive for TE-ADAs. Among subjects who received the proposed dosing regimen of garadacimab (400 mg SC loading dose on Day 1, then 200 mg SC once monthly) in trial 3001 and study 3002 (n=129; includes 39 garadacimab-treated subjects in 3001, 21 placebo-treated subjects in 3001 who rolled over to 3002, and 69 garadacimab-naïve subjects from 3002), the incidence of TE-ADAs was 3.9 % (5/129). Of six post-treatment ADA-positive samples, only two samples showed higher titers (160 and 320), whereas four samples showed lower titers (10). In addition, only one out of five TE-ADA positive subjects tested positive at more than one time point, indicating that most positive immunogenicity results were transient. In phase 1 single-dose studies conducted in healthy subjects (i.e., 1001, 1003, and 1004), no subjects tested positive for ADAs.
L1509: Note that the drug tolerance of the ADA assay is adequate up to 30 µg/mL garadacimab (refer to Office of Pharmaceutical Quality executive summary review dated August 26, 2024). In trial 3001, no subjects had garadacimab trough concentrations exceeding 30 µg/mL with median (range) trough concentrations from Day 60 through Day 180 ranging from 7.21 (1.26, 21.3) µg/mL to 8.27 (1.75, 17.9) µg/mL. In study 3002 at ADA sample collection time points, five subjects had garadacimab concentrations exceeding 30 µg/mL, including two subjects at Month 6 (concentrations of 34.1 and 37.7 µg/mL), and three subjects at Month 12 (concentrations of 31.9 to 33.4 µg/mL). There were few samples with garadacimab concentrations exceeding 30 µg/mL. As the ADA incidence was generally low, these observations are not expected to impact the overall assessment.
L1510: A summary of immunogenicity data are provided in Table 97. TE-ADAs were observed in trial 3001 and study 3002 among subjects receiving the proposed TBM dosage. In trial 3001, one
L1511: subject tested positive for TE-ADAs on Day 182. This subject also tested positive during study 3002 at the Month 6 visit. The remaining four subjects tested positive during study 3002. Pretreatment ADAs were detected in an additional two subjects, one from trial 2001 and one from trial 3001.

L1538: In two TE-ADA-positive subjects ( (b) (6) ), no treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) were reported. One TE-ADA-positive subject ( (b) (6) ) reported three TEAEs, all of which were judged as not related to treatment, including upper respiratory tract infection, increased blood creatinine, and COVID-19. All TEAEs occurred prior to detection of ADAs. One TE-ADA-positive subject ( (b) (6) ) reported four TEAEs, including injection site pruritus, injection site erythema, pyrexia, and myalgia. Only the injection site reactions were deemed related to treatment. All TEAEs occurred prior to detection of ADAs. The remaining TE-ADA-positive subject ( (b) (6) ), who tested positive for ADAs at the End of Treatment visit, discontinued from the study due to injection site irritation of moderate intensity deemed related to treatment. Prior to detection of ADAs, this subject also experienced treatment-related AEs of erythema and injection site erythema. Notably, this subject also presented with the highest titer ADAs.
L1539: In general, TE-ADAs did not impact the safety of garadacimab. For one subject who discontinued prematurely due to moderate injection site irritation and presented with the highest titer ADAs, the impact of ADAs on safety cannot be ruled out. Overall, few subjects with HAE
L1540: tested positive for ADAs across the garadacimab development program, precluding a definitive assessment of the impact of ADAs on safety.
L1541: ## 14.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment
L1542: ## 14.5.1. Population PK Analysis
L1543: ## 14.5.1.1. Executive Summary
L1544: The Applicant submitted a population PK report entitled “Population PK-PD Modeling and Simulation of Garadacimab in Subjects with Hereditary Angioedema” to support the application of garadacimab for the treatment of adolescents and adults with HAE. The population PK and PK-PD models in this report were developed using data from two phase 1 studies, one phase 2 study, and two phase 3 studies.
L1545: In general, the Applicant’s population PK and PK-PD models adequately characterize the PK/PD (FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity) of garadacimab, and the model-predicted real time concentrations of garadacimab are adequate for use in E-R analysis. In the final model, PK was described by a two-compartment model with first-order absorption and elimination, and PD was described by a direct effect sigmoidal maximal-efficacy model. None of the covariates (e.g., age, sex, race, body weight, renal impairment, Japanese ethnicity, Chinese ethnicity, baseline serum creatinine, baseline alanine aminotransferase, baseline bilirubin, baseline FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity) were likely to have clinically meaningful impact on garadacimab exposure and PD response. The recommended dosage for garadacimab in patients with HAE is supported by the population PK/PK-PD analyses results as well as those of the other analyses (i.e., clinical efficacy, safety, and E-R analyses).

L1548: General Information
Objectives of PPK analysis	Develop a population pharmacokinetic (PK) model with pooled data from study 1001, study 1003, trial 2001, trial 3001, and study 3002.
Evaluate the impact of specific covariates on PK and assess the need for dose-adjustments for certain subgroups of patients.
Develop a population pharmacokinetic-pharmacodynamic (PKPD) model for FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity with data from study 1001, study 1003, trial 2001, trial 3001, and study 3002.
Evaluate the impact of specific covariates on pharmacodynamics (PD).
Perform simulations to confirm dose selection.

Study included	Study 1001, study 1003, trial 2001, trial 3001, and study 3002

L1551: General Information
Model structure	
PopPK
Two-compartment model with first-order absorption and elimination.
The effects of body weight on all clearance and volume terms were estimated.
The effects of Japanese ethnicity (Japanese versus non-Japanese), Chinese ethnicity (Chinese versus non-Chinese), subject status (healthy versus HAE subject), baseline serum creatinine (SCR), baseline alanine aminotransferase (ALT) and baseline bilirubin (BILI) on clearance were estimated.
IIV was estimated on CL, V2 and F1 with a full \Omega block.
Residual variability included a proportional term.
PopPK-PD
A direct effect sigmoidal E_{max} model.
The effect of baseline FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity on the EC_{50} and E_0 parameters was estimated.
IIV was estimated on EC_{50}, E_0 and GAMMA.
Residual variability included a proportional term and an additive term.
Acceptable
Model parameter estimates	
PopPK: Table 102
PopPK-PD: Table 103
Acceptable
Uncertainty and variability (RSE, IIV, shrinkage, bootstrap)	
PopPK
The RSE for all parameters except SCR on CL were <50%. The RSE for SCR on CL was >100%. Shrinkage (%) on IIV for CL, V2, and F1 are 28.7, 25.2, and 43.3, respectively.
PopPK-PD
The RSE for all parameters except EC_{50}-E_0 covariance and GAMMA-EC_{50} covariance were <30%. The RSE for all the parameters were <50%. Eta-shrinkage for EC_{50}, E_0, and GAMMA were 20.2%, 14.7%, and 27%.
Acceptable. The high RSE for SCR on CL suggested the effect of SCR cannot be estimated precisely with the available data. The RSE for other parameters are reasonable.
BLQ for parameter accuracy	BLQ PK data and BLQ PD data were disregarded.	Acceptable

L1609: The Applicant's population PK/PK-PD analyses are acceptable. Overall, the final population PK and PK-PD models are adequate to characterize the PK/PD profile of garadacimab as indicated in the Applicant's diagnostic plots. The FDA reviewer was able to repeat and verify the Applicant's analysis with no significant discordance identified. The population PK model excluding data from study 1001, which had an issue in the bioanalysis of samples, did not suggest any changes in conclusions regarding the intrinsic factors in the proposed drug label. The reviewer agrees with the Applicant's conclusion regarding the effect of covariates on garadacimab exposure and FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity. FDA accepted the labeling language in Section 12.3 of the prescribing information (PI) related to PK parameters and effect of covariates (age, race, body weight, renal impairment, (b) (4))

L1614: ## 14.5.2. Exposure-Response Analysis
L1615: ## 14.5.2.1. ER (Efficacy) Executive Summary
L1616: A time-varying Poisson process model with a maximum inhibition (E-R) relationship generally described the efficacy endpoint (HAE attack rate) in subjects with HAE who received either garadacimab or placebo in the phase 2 trial (2001) and phase 3 studies (3001 and 3002). None of the covariates (e.g., age, Japanese, Chinese, male, prior treatment, the use of concomitant medications [analgesic, anti-inflammatory and antirheumatic, antihistamine, antibacterials] and rescue medications [plasma-derived and recombinant C1-INH or icatibant], body weight, obesity) were likely to have clinically meaningful impact on the effectiveness of garadacimab in reducing HAE attack. Dose adjustments due to the forementioned covariates are not needed.
L1617: ## 14.5.2.2. ER (Efficacy) Assessment Summary
L1618: **Table 105. ER (Efficacy) Summary**
L1619: General Information
Goal of ER analysis	
Develop an exposure-response (ER) model for garadacimab and hereditary angioedema (HAE) attacks with data from trial 2001, trial 3001, and study 3002
Explore effects of covariates on the HAE attack rate and/or drug activity.
Explore the impact of rescue medication use on the HAE attack rate.
Perform simulations to confirm efficacy of a 400 mg (2×200 mg) subcutaneous (SC) loading dose followed by 200 mg once monthly SC dose.

Study included	Trial 2001, trial 3001, and study 3002
Endpoint	HAE attack rate
No. of subjects (total, and with individual pharmacokinetics [PK])	177
Population characteristics
(Table 106)	
General
Age: median 41 yr (range 12-73 yr)
Weight: median 79.2 kg (range 43.3-153 kg)
64 (36.2%) male
147 (83.1%) White, 3 (1.7%) black, of African heritage or African American, 24 (13.6%) Asian, 3 (1.7%) other
Pediatrics (if any)
Age: median 15 yr (range: 12-17 yr, 0% subj ≤2 yr, 0% subj ≤6 yr, 9.1% subj ≤12 yr)
Weight: median 57.8 kg (range: 43.3-153.2 kg)

Dose(s) included	40 mg IV loading followed by 75 mg Q4W SC;
100 mg IV loading followed by 200 mg Q4W SC;
300 mg IV loading followed by 600 mg Q4W SC;
400 mg Q2W SC;
200 or 600 mg Q4W SC;
400 mg SC loading followed by 200 mg monthly SC

Exposure metrics explored (range)	Real time concentration
L1620: General Information
Covariates evaluated	Baseline age, baseline weight, sex, ethnicity (Japanese versus non-Japanese, Asian versus non-Asian, Chinese versus non-Chinese), HAE subtypes (healthy, Type I, Type II, FXII/PLG HAE), Baseline attack rate, HAE prophylaxis treatment used prior to study entry, Class of HAE acute treatment used prior to study entry (three drug classes: complement C1 esterase inhibitors, B2 receptor antagonists (icatibant acetate), and other rescue medication)
Final Model Parameters
Summary	Acceptability [FDA's Comments]
Model structure	A time-varying Poisson process model with a maximum inhibition exposure-response relationship	Acceptable
Model parameter estimates	Table 107	Acceptable
Model evaluation	Figure 57	Acceptable
Covariates and clinical relevance	Figure 58	Acceptable
Simulation for specific population	Not applicable	N/A
Visualization of E-R relationships	Figure 59	Acceptable
Overall clinical relevance for ER	Population simulations based on the final model suggested the monthly HAE attack rate and HAE relative risk decreased with increasing exposure (AUC_{\tau_{au,ss}}, C_{min,ss}, and C_{max,ss}). More than 70% of subjects who received 200 mg SC monthly maintenance dose were projected to have 90% reduction in relative risk at steady state; more than 85% subjects who received 400 mg SC loading dose were projected to have 90% reduction in relative risk after the first dose. The recommended dose regimen as implemented in trial 3001 and study 3002 is appropriate for prevention1 of HAE attacks in subjects 12 yr and older.	Acceptable
Labeling Language
Description	Acceptability [FDA's Comments]

L1668: ## 15. Additional Study/Trial Design Information
L1669: ## 15.1. Trial 3001: A Multicenter, Double-Blind, Randomized, Placebo-Controlled, Parallel-Arm Study To Investigate the Efficacy and Safety of Subcutaneous Administration of CSL312 (Garadacimab) in the Prophylactic Treatment of Hereditary Angioedema
L1670: Applicant:	CSL Behring, LLC
Drug name:	CSL312 (garadacimab)
Indication:	Prophylaxis to prevent attacks of HAE in subjects 12 years and older
Source of information:	CSL312_3001 Clinical Study Protocol (dated August 4, 2020); CSL312_3001 Clinical Study Report (dated October 6, 2022; submitted June 28, 2023); CSL312_3001 Statistical Analysis Plan (dated April 21, 2021)
Trial identifier:	VANGUARD
Protocol number:	CSL312_3001
Clinical phase:	Phase 3
EudraCT number:	2020-000570-25
IND number:	139936
ClinicalTrial.gov identifier:	NCT 04656418
Ethics:	The clinical study protocol and all amendments were approved by the independent ethics committees/institutional review boards of the participating centers. The study was carried out in accordance with the ICH of Technical Requirements for Pharmaceuticals for Human Use (ICH) Good Clinical Practice guidelines, and the ethical principles that have their origin in the Declaration of Helsinki.
Trial centers:	Germany, Israel, United States, Canada, Netherlands, Japan, and Hungary
Planned duration of main phase:	6 months
Trial status:	Completed
Trial start date:	January 27, 2021
Trial completion date:	June 7, 2022
L1671: ## 15.1.1. Definition and Collection of HAE Attacks, Trial 3001
L1672: The following items pertaining to an acute HAE attack event, including start and end dates and times, were recorded by subjects during the study in an eDiary: HAE attack onset and resolution, HAE symptoms (with location), use of on-demand therapy or medical assistance to treat HAE symptoms, and interference with daily activities. Participants were encouraged to notify and report HAE attacks to the study site within 72 h of symptom onset. HAE attacks were confirmed by the investigator after reviewing the eDiary data at each visit and collecting any additional missing or relevant information. Per the clinical study protocol (dated August 4, 2020), investigator-confirmed HAE attacks were defined as meeting all of the following criteria:
L1673: - Associated with at least one symptom or location (see Table 108).
- Separated from the previous attack by a minimum of 24 h without symptoms.
- Consisted of symptoms typical of an HAE attack and not just prodromal symptoms.
- Lacked an alternative cause for the symptoms.
- Lacked use of on-demand medication to only treat prodromal symptoms.

L1677: ## 15.1.2. Data and Safety Monitoring Committee, Trial 3001
L1678: An independent data monitoring committee (IDMC) was established to monitor the safety and efficacy data generated during the trial. The IDMC was comprised of an independent, unblinded statistician and independent clinical specialists in the fields of HAE management who have experience in clinical trials. The IDMC reviewed accumulating data during the study in an unblinded manner to advise on the further conduct of the study. No success or futility thresholds were established for IDMC reviews (the Applicant would not stop the study unless a major safety issue was identified). No safety concerns were identified during the IDMC meetings, and the IDMC recommended that the study continue as planned.
L1679: ## 15.1.3. Sample Size Considerations, Trial 3001
L1680: The Applicant determined that 40 subjects (allocated 3:2 to garadacimab: placebo) with C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE completing the 6-month Treatment Period would be needed to achieve a power of approximately 90% via a two-sided Wilcoxon test at \alpha=5\%. Monthly HAE attack rates of 0.3125 and 1.3 were assumed for the garadacimab and placebo arms (equivalent to a 76% reduction relative to placebo) and were assumed to be Poisson distributed (the assumed monthly attack rates were used as the two means of the Poisson distributions).
L1681: The assumed attack rate for the garadacimab arm was based on the results of Treatment Period 1 in the phase 2 trial (2001; see Section 16.1), where the mean of the individual attack rates for the 24 subjects who were administered 75 mg, 200 mg, or 600 mg garadacimab was calculated as 0.3125 attacks per month. To be conservative, the mean attack rate of the three pooled doses was used instead of the mean attack rate for only the 200 mg garadacimab arm, as the 200 mg garadacimab arm showed the highest treatment effect among the three doses. The assumed attack rate for the placebo arm was based on a publication of a prior phase 3 study of lanadelumab (not conducted by the Applicant) for the prevention of HAE attacks (Banerji et al. 2018), where an attack rate of 0.3 attacks per week was assumed (which is equivalent to 1.3 attacks per month).
L1682: The Applicant performed the sample size calculation for this study using the “proc power” procedure in SAS (version 9.4), which uses the O’Brien-Castelloe approach to compute the approximate power. The randomization ratio of 3:2 was implemented by using “GROUPNS=(24 16)”, resulting in a total sample size of 40, which was the lowest one reaching a power of 90%.
L1683: To allow for sufficient safety data, to increase the likelihood of adolescents entering the study, and to have at least 40 subjects reaching the end of the study, an additional 20 subjects were planned to be randomized (for a total planned sample size of about 60 subjects). The Applicant also aimed to randomize approximately 5 adolescent subjects and approximately 5 Japanese subjects among the 60 total subjects.
L1684: The study included a total of 64 subjects (39 on garadacimab and 25 on placebo) and met the Applicant’s main enrollment goal (though one subject on placebo was excluded from efficacy analyses due to receiving less than 30 days of study treatment). The Applicant also met their other enrollment goals (6 adolescent subjects and 6 Japanese subjects were included in the study).
L1685: The sample size for this phase 3 trial was comparable to prior similar HAE programs and was generally reasonable, as HAE is a rare condition.
L1686: ## 15.1.4. Changes to the Planned Analyses, Trial 3001
L1687: The Applicant made the following changes prior to database lock in relation to the prespecified analyses in the original clinical study protocol (dated August 4, 2020):
L1688: - Estimand definitions were updated to reflect the potential impact of the COVID-19 pandemic. No subjects discontinued treatment or the study as a result of the COVID-19 pandemic.
- Comparison of the distribution of responses to therapy (via the Subject's Global Assessment of Response to Therapy (SGART)) between the garadacimab and placebo arms at the end of the 6-month Treatment Period used data at Visit Day 182 only. A two-sided chi-squared test at \alpha=5\% was used. If a subject's SGART value at Visit Day 182 was missing, then the subject was not included in the exploratory analysis.
- Definitions for H02 and H04 were modified.
- For the primary estimand, the intercurrent event of early discontinuation due or not due to COVID-19 was removed.
L1689: The Applicant removed this intercurrent event per FDA feedback, as the Agency did not consider this to be an intercurrent event.
L1690: - Exploratory analyses of patient-reported outcome endpoints (the angioedema quality of life, EQ-5D-5L, Work Productivity and Activity Impairment, and Investigator's Global Assessment of Response to Therapy) were conducted in the intent-to-treat (ITT) Analysis Set rather than the Safety Analysis Set.
L1691: No changes were made to the Applicant's prespecified analyses after database lock. However, the following additional analyses were conducted after database lock:
L1692: - The time-normalized number of HAE attacks within 3 months before Screening for subjects with and without HAE prophylaxis during 3 months before Screening (for HAE history using the ITT Analysis Set).
- The time-normalized number of HAE attacks during the 3 months before Screening or start of HAE prophylaxis (for HAE history using the ITT Analysis Set).
- The time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month for the first and second 3 months of treatment in the ITT Analysis Set using a generalized linear model for count data assuming a Poisson distribution.
- The time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month requiring on-demand therapy in the ITT Analysis Set using a Poisson modeled structured similarly to the Applicant's sensitivity analysis for the primary endpoint described in Section 6.2.3.2, where the baseline attack rate only included HAE attacks that required on-demand therapy during the Run-in Period.
- The time-normalized number of moderate or severe HAE attacks per month in the ITT Analysis Set using a Poisson modeled structured similarly to the Applicant's sensitivity analysis for the primary endpoint described in Section 6.2.3.2, where the baseline attack rate only included HAE attacks that were moderate or severe during the Run-in Period.
L1693: The results of analyses #4 and #5 above were submitted by the Applicant at the Agency's request during the review cycle. The results of these analyses can be found in Section 6.2.4.5.
L1694: ## 15.1.5. Protocol Amendments, Trial 3001
L1695: The original clinical study protocol (dated August 4, 2020) had no substantial amendments and had one administrative amendment (dated December 8, 2020) that only added clarification of an exclusion criterion related to use of contraception. A Japanese version of the protocol (dated January 15, 2021) was created to include study sites in Japan, which contained several minor administrative adjustments required for compliance with local regulations in Japan.
L1696: None of the above changes substantially impacted the study design or results.
L1697: ## 15.1.6. Safety Assessments, Trial 3001
L1698: Safety assessments included AE reporting, laboratory parameters (clinical chemistry, hematology, coagulation, urinalysis), vital signs, physical examinations, and EKG testing according to the Schedules of Assessments (see Figure 60 and Figure 61). Labs were collected at baseline and Months 3 and 6. Subjects had an additional lab collection on Day 1 if baseline labs were collected more than 90 days prior to the first dose of garadacimab administration. Pregnancy tests, urinalyses, and hematology, chemistry, and coagulation labs were processed at a central laboratory. Overall, the safety monitoring in trial 3001 was adequate to inform the safety profile of garadacimab for the proposed use.

L1726: d Subjects who did not enter Study CSL312_3002 were required to complete the Follow-up Visit. The in-person Follow-up Visit could have been waived in place of a telephone contact during which information about adverse events and concomitant therapies / medications was collected. If the Follow-up Visit was conducted as an in-person visit, then a blood draw for assessment of PK / PD and immunogenicity occurred, as well as collection of information about adverse events, concomitant therapies / medications.
L1727: e Subjects were eligible to exit in the Run-in Period and enter the Treatment Period after completing at least 1 month of the Run-in Period and experiencing at least 2 HAE attacks. C1-INH functional activity and antigen, and C4 antigen concentration levels were confirmed before randomization into the Treatment Period. Eligibility was confirmed before randomization and any study assessments were performed.
L1728: f The urine test for beta-human chorionic gonadotropin was performed for sexually active women of childbearing potential (only). A serum pregnancy test was performed by the site if urine result was inconclusive.
L1729: g A physical examination was conducted per the investigator's standard procedure and also included assessment of unilateral pain and / or swelling of the lower extremities for the purpose of screening for deep vein thrombosis.
L1730: h At visits, administration of investigational product occurred after physical examinations, vital signs, body weight, and pregnancy tests were completed.
L1731: i Standard 12-lead ECGs were performed.
L1732: j Assessments could have been omitted if Screening / Run-in Period assessments occurred within the previous 3 months.
L1733: k Blood samples for potential future assessment of HAE biomarkers were obtained during the Treatment Period. These samples were stored for potential testing of HAE biomarkers and will be destroyed within 5 years after completion of the study or used for future research on HAE. These samples were not collected in adolescents.
L1734: l Blood sample for PK included CSL312 concentration and blood sample for PD included FXII concentration and FXIIa-mediated kallikrein activity.
L1735: m Blood sample for binding antibodies (inhibitory and non-inhibitory) specific to FXIIa inhibitor monoclonal antibody (anti-CSL312).
L1736: n The following patient-related outcomes were entered at specified study site visits before any other interaction with the subject about their health status and before review of the eDiary: Angioedema QoL, EQ-5D-5L, WPAI:GH, and SGART.
L1737: o Investigator completed IGART in RAVE. Subjects completed SGART.
L1738: p Only applicable for subjects who did not roll over to Study CSL312_3002.
L1739: q The eDiary was collected and deactivated on Day 182 for subjects rolling over to Study CSL312_3002 and on Day 242 for all other subjects. For subjects who terminated the study early, the eDiary was deactivated and collected either on date of early termination or at the Follow-up Visit as applicable. If the Follow-up Visit was conducted remotely, the device should have been returned to the site.
L1740: r Berinert was the suggested on-demand medication for use during this study. Subjects could use the on-demand medication of their choice to treat HAE attacks experienced during the study if that medication had previously been shown to be effective.
L1741: s The first dose of investigational product was a loading dose consisting of either 2 SC injections of 200 mg CSL312 each for a total of 400 mg CSL312 (Active Arm) or 2 SC injections of volume-matched placebo (Placebo Arm) administered by the investigator or delegate at the study site.
L1742: t Investigational product administration consisting of either 1 200 mg CSL312 SC injection (Active Arm) or 1 SC injection of volume-matched placebo (Placebo Arm) could have been self-administered by the subject under the supervision of the investigator or delegate at the study site.
L1743: Source: Phase 3 Clinical Trial Protocol, p. 13.
L1744: ## 15.1.7. Grading of Adverse Events, Trial 3001
L1745: Table 109. Grading Scale for Adverse Event Severity in Trial 3001
L1746: Severity	Definition
Mild	A type of AE that is usually transient and may require only minimal treatment or therapeutic intervention. The event does not generally interfere with usual activities of daily living.
Moderate	A type of AE that is usually alleviated with additional specific therapeutic intervention. The event interferes with usual activities of daily living, causing discomfort but poses no significant or permanent risk of harm to the subject.
Severe	A type of AE that interrupts usual activities of daily living, or significantly affects clinical status, or may require intensive therapeutic intervention.
L1747: Source: CDISC SDTM Severity Intensity Scale for Adverse Event Terminology, phase 3 Clinical Trial Protocol, p. 80.
Abbreviations: AE, adverse event; CDISC, Clinical Data Interchange Standards Consortium; SDTM, Study Data Tabulation Model
L1748: ## 15.2. Study 3002: An Open-Label Study To Evaluate the Long-Term Safety and Efficacy of CSL312 (Garadacimab) in the Prophylactic Treatment of Hereditary Angioedema
L1749: Applicant:	CSL Behring, LLC
Drug name:	Garadacimab
Indication:	Prophylaxis to prevent attacks of HAE in subjects 12 years and older
Source of information:	CSL312_3002 Interim Clinical Study Report (dated August 10, 2023); CSL312_3002 Statistical Analysis Plan (dated June 25, 2021)
Trial identifiers	
Protocol number:	CSL312_3002
Clinical phase:	Phase 3b (open-label study)
EudraCT number:	2020-003918-12
IND number:	139936
ClinicalTrial.gov identifier:	NCT04739059
Ethics:	The clinical study protocol and all amendments were approved by the independent ethics committees/institutional review boards of the participating centers. The study was carried out in accordance with the ICH of Technical Requirements for Pharmaceuticals for Human Use (ICH) Good Clinical Practice guidelines.
Trial centers:	Australia (4 centers), Canada (5 centers), Czech Republic (2 centers), Germany (5 centers), Hong Kong (1 center), Hungary (1 center), Israel (1 center), Japan (10 centers), Netherlands (1 center), New Zealand (1 center), Russia (1 center), Spain (2 centers), Taiwan (1 center), and the United States (12 centers)
Planned duration of main phase:	At least 12 months
Trial status:	Ongoing
L1750: Applicant:	CSL Behring, LLC
Trial start date:	March 29, 2021
Trial completion date:	November 2025 (anticipated)
Date of database lock:	February 13, 2023 (all subjects except adolescents);
May 22, 2023 (adolescents only)
L1751: ## 15.2.1. Study Design, Study 3002
L1752: Study 3002 was a multicenter, open-label, phase 3b study to investigate the long-term safety and efficacy of garadacimab when administered once monthly for at least 12 months. Subjects eligible to participate in this study were:
L1753: - Subjects from the phase 2 trial (2001).
- Subjects from the phase 3 trial (3001).
- Garadacimab-naïve subjects (i.e., newly enrolled subjects with C1-INH [Type I or Type II] HAE).
L1754: The study comprised four periods (see Figure 62):
L1755: - Screening Period (up to 1 month; for garadacimab-naïve subjects).
- Run-in Period (up to 2 months; for garadacimab-naïve subjects).
- Treatment Period (12 months minimum; open-label).
- Follow-up Period (2 months after End of Treatment [EoT] Visit).
L1756: Figure 62. Study Schematic, Study 3002
L1757: Source: Interim Clinical Study Report (dated August 10, 2023), Figure 9-1, p. 19.
Abbreviations: CSL312, garadacimab; EoT, end of treatment; HAE, hereditary angioedema; OL, open-label
L1758: Following informed consent, garadacimab-naïve subjects underwent a Screening Period of up to 1 month to determine eligibility for enrollment in the study. Screened subjects who met all inclusion criteria, and none of the exclusion criteria, entered the Run-in Period. If a subject did not meet the criteria for entering the Run-in Period within 30 days, the subject could have been rescreened once.
L1759: After Screening, eligible subjects entered the Run-in Period lasting at least 1 month and up to 2 months to confirm underlying disease status and to assess eligibility for participation in the Treatment Period. The first day of the Run-in Period could have occurred on the same day as Screening. Subjects had to experience \ge 2 HAE attacks during the Run-in Period to be eligible to enter the Treatment Period. Subjects who experienced \ge 2 HAE attacks during the required first month of the Run-in Period could immediately enter the Treatment Period. Subjects who did not experience an HAE attack during the first month of the Run-in Period remained in the Run-in Period for up to 2 months, during which time they were required to experience \ge 2 HAE attacks to be eligible to enter the Treatment Period. If a subject was unable to enter the Treatment Period after Day 60, then approval from the Applicant was required for the subject to extend the Run-in Period for another month to enter the Treatment Period within 3 months. Subjects were prohibited from using routine prophylaxis to prevent HAE attacks during the Run-in Period; however, subjects could have used on-demand HAE therapy (i.e., C1-INH replacement or icatibant or ecallantide) to treat HAE attacks if the medication was previously shown to be effective. Subjects who did not meet the minimum HAE attack rate during the Run-in Period or were otherwise determined to be ineligible due to Screening assessments were considered Run-in failures and were not rescreened.
L1760: Subjects meeting all eligibility criteria entered the Treatment Period after the Run-in Period. If a subject was unable to enter the Treatment Period after Day 60, then approval from the Applicant was required for the subject to enter the Treatment Period. During the Treatment Period, subjects were assigned to 200 mg garadacimab administered SC once per month. For garadacimab-naïve subjects, a loading dose of 400 mg garadacimab (two 200 mg doses) was administered SC on Visit Day 1, then 200 mg per month in subsequent months. The Treatment Period had a variable duration for individual subjects; however, each subject received garadacimab for at least 12 months.
L1761: For the Follow-up Period, subjects were contacted approximately two months after their EoT Visit to complete final study assessments.
L1762: For details regarding the collection and reporting of HAE data, and the investigator confirmation of HAE attacks, see Section 15.1.1.
L1763: ## 15.2.1.1. Sample Size Assumptions, Study 3002
L1764: Approximately 150 subjects (including 8 Japanese subjects) were planned to be enrolled in the study, with a minimum of 100 subjects having at least 12 months of exposure to garadacimab. Since this was an open label, single-arm study with no control arm, the sample size for this study was not based on a formal calculation but on the International Council for Harmonisation guidance, *E1A The Extent of Population Exposure to Assess Clinical Safety for Drugs Intended for Long-Term Treatment of Non-Life-Threatening Conditions* (October 1995). The Applicant claimed that a sample size of 100 subjects would allow for the observation of \ge 1 AE with a probability of 3% at a 95% confidence level.
L1765: ## 15.2.2. Schedule of Assessments, Study 3002
L1766: During the Treatment Period of study 3002, subjects received garadacimab 200 mg SC every month. Study visits, vitals, physical examinations, laboratory monitoring, and AE monitoring for
L1767: rollovers and garadacimab-naïve subjects are shown in Figure 63 and Figure 64. The trial 3001 rollovers and garadacimab-naïve subjects received laboratory assessments on Day 1, Month 3, Month 6, Month 12, and every 6 months thereafter (Figure 63). The trial 2001 rollover subjects received laboratory assessments on Day 1, Month 6, Month 12, and every 6 months thereafter (Figure 64). The laboratory assessments were collected at 6-month intervals for the trial 2001 rollovers as those subjects had already received garadacimab every 4 weeks for about 1 year. The laboratory analyses were conducted at a central laboratory. The schedules of assessments are acceptable for a phase 3, open-label extension study.

L1805: ## 15.2.3. Primary, Secondary and Exploratory Objectives and Endpoints, Study 3002
L1806: The primary objective of this study was to evaluate the long-term safety of garadacimab in the prophylactic treatment of subjects with C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE.
L1807: **Primary Endpoint:** frequency of TEAEs.
L1808: **Secondary Endpoints:** The secondary objectives of this study were to evaluate long-term safety and efficacy, including patient-reported assessment of response to therapy.
L1809: - Serious adverse events
- Deaths
- Related TEAEs
- TEAEs leading to study discontinuation
- TEAEs by severity
- Anti-garadacimab antibodies
- Laboratory findings reported as an AE
- Adverse events of special interest (thromboembolic events, abnormal bleeding events, and severe hypersensitivity including anaphylaxis events)
- TEAEs among C1-INH subjects
- Time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month
- Reduction in the monthly attack rate during the Treatment Period compared to the Run-in Period
- Time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month requiring on-demand treatment
- Time-normalized number of moderate and/or severe HAE attacks per month
- SGART responses of *good* or *excellent* at the end of the Treatment Period
L1810: The exploratory objectives of this study were to evaluate the efficacy and quality-of-life (QoL) associated with the use of garadacimab in subjects with HAE. PK and PD were also assessed.
L1811: ## **Exploratory Endpoints**
L1812: - Time to first HAE attack after Day-1 administration (after Day-14 administration for garadacimab-naïve subjects).
- Quality-of-life questionnaires:Angioedema Quality of Life Questionnaire.
- Work Productivity and Activity Impairment: General Health.
- Treatment Satisfaction for Medication Questionnaire II.
- Investigator's Global Assessment of Response to Therapy.
- PK/PD parameters.
L1813: ## **15.2.4. Analysis Populations, Study 3002**
L1814: The following analysis sets were defined by the Applicant in the final statistical analysis plan (statistical analysis plan (SAP); version 1.0; dated June 25, 2021):
L1815: Screened Analysis Set: All subjects who provided written informed consent.
L1816: All Treated Subjects Analysis Set: All subjects in the Screened Analysis Set who were assigned to treatment. The All-Treated Subjects (ATS) Analysis Set was analyzed using the treatment to which the subject was assigned, regardless of the treatment actually received. This was the main set used for efficacy analyses.
L1817: Safety Analysis Set: All subjects in the ATS Analysis Set who received at least 1 dose of investigational product. The Safety Analysis Set was analyzed using the actual treatment received (the actual treatment was planned to be garadacimab for all subjects, but the dose level could have been incremented from the initially planned 200 mg). This was the main set used for safety analyses.
L1818: Per Protocol Analysis Set: All subjects in the ATS Analysis Set who received at least one dose of investigational product and who complied with the protocol. Decisions regarding exclusion from the Per Protocol Analysis Set were planned to be made and documented before database lock.
L1819: ## 15.2.5. Time Point Description, Study 3002
L1820: AEs, HAE attacks, laboratory data, vital signs, and other measurements were assigned to the Applicant-defined study periods shown below in Table 110.
L1821: **Table 110. Definitions of Study Periods (Study 3002)**
L1822: Period	Start Date	End Date
Screening Period	Date of Informed Consent	Run-in Period Start Date
Run-in Period	Run-in Period Start Date	Day 1 Visit date of the Treatment Period or Date of Run-in Non-completion [whichever is first]
Treatment/Efficacy evaluation period	Date of Day 1 Visit of Treatment Period	Date of End of Treatment Visit or End of Study date [whichever is first]
Treatment/Efficacy evaluation period for uptitrated doses	Date of Day 1 Visit of Treatment Period (for 200mg CSL312) or Date of Dose Uptitration (for 400mg or 600 mg CSL312, respectively)	Date of next Dose Uptitration (from CRF) – 1 Day or Date of End of Treatment Visit or End of Study date [whichever is first]
Follow-up	Date of End of Treatment Visit	Date of Final/ Follow-up Visit
Safety Evaluation Period	Date and start time of First CSL312 Administration as entered in the eDiary	Date of Final/ Follow-up Visit
L1823: Source: Statistical Analysis Plan (version 1.0; dated June 25, 2021), Table 3, p. 27.
L1824: Abbreviations: CRF, case report form; CSL312, garadacimab
L1825: The efficacy periods for up-titrated doses only existed for subjects who had a dose increase. The safety evaluation period consisted of the Treatment/Efficacy evaluation period plus the Follow-
L1826: up period. Screening and Run-in periods were not performed for rollover subjects (instead, relevant endpoint data, such as HAE attacks during the Run-in Period, were used from trials 2001 and 3001). The durations of the Treatment/Efficacy and Safety evaluation periods (in days) were calculated as: End Date – Start Date + 1 Day. The durations of the Screening, Run-in, and Follow-up periods (in days) were calculated as: End Date – Start Date.
L1827: ## 15.2.6. Primary Analysis Description, Study 3002
L1828: The primary endpoint of TEAEs was provided as number of subjects, percentage of subjects, and number of events. The TEAE rates per injection and per subject-year were also reported.
L1829: ## 15.2.7. Secondary Efficacy Analysis Description, Study 3002
L1830: The secondary efficacy endpoints were analyzed using the ATS Analysis Set. The time-normalized number of HAE attacks was also analyzed using the Per Protocol Analysis Set.
L1831: For all analyses considering HAE attacks, only investigator-confirmed attacks were used. Subjects entered HAE symptoms into an eDiary (with the start and end date and time), along with location of HAE symptoms, use of on-demand therapy to treat HAE symptoms, and interference of HAE symptoms with daily activities. The investigator confirmed HAE attacks based on a review of the eDiary, relevant medical history, and physician judgment.
L1832: For garadacimab-naïve subjects, HAE attacks with a start date on or after the first day of the Run-in Period but prior to the date and time of the first study drug administration were counted for the Run-in Period. For subjects from the phase 2 (2001) or phase 3 (3001) trial, the number of HAE attacks during the Run-in Period of the previous study was used. HAE attacks with a start date and time after the first study drug administration through the EoT Visit were counted for the Treatment Period and were included in the efficacy analyses.
L1833: The time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month were calculated per subject as:
L1834: [\text{number of HAE attacks} \div \text{length of subject treatment in days}] \times 30.4375
L1835: Length of subject treatment in days was calculated as the duration of the Treatment/Efficacy evaluation period defined in Table 110.
L1836: The time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month were summarized descriptively using the mean and median with corresponding 95% CIs.
L1837: Reduction in HAE attack rate during the Treatment Period compared to the Run-in Period was summarized descriptively as a percent reduction per subject, calculated as:
L1838: 100\% \times (1 - [\text{time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month during treatment} \div \text{time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month during the Run-in Period}]).
L1839: The number and percentage of responders and nonresponders were also presented with corresponding 95% CIs. A subject was classified as a responder if their percent reduction in HAE attacks during treatment compared to the Run-in Period was \ge 50\%. The number and percentage of subjects with reductions of \ge 70\%, \ge 90\%, and 100\% (i.e., attack-free during
L1840: treatment) were also reported with associated 95% CIs. Exact Clopper-Pearson CIs were used for all percentages.
L1841: The time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month requiring on-demand therapy was summarized descriptively, calculated per subject as:
L1842: \frac{\text{number of HAE attacks requiring on-demand therapy}}{\text{length of subject treatment in days}} \times 30.4375.
L1843: Length of subject treatment in days was calculated as the duration of the Treatment/Efficacy evaluation period defined in Table 110.
L1844: The time-normalized number of moderate and/or severe HAE attacks per month was calculated and analyzed similarly to the total time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month (described above).
L1845: Finally, SGART results were presented descriptively by study visit where assessed. The number and percentage of subjects with each rating (“none”, “poor”, “fair”, “good”, or “excellent”) were reported, along with cumulative responses (“poor or better”, “fair or better”, and “good or excellent”). A two-sided 95% CI was reported for the percentages for each cumulative response category. An exploratory Friedman test at \alpha=5\% was conducted to compare the percentage of subjects with responses of “good” or “excellent” between Visit Day 361 and Visit Day 721.
L1846: ## 15.2.8. Sensitivity and Supportive Statistical Analyses Description, Study 3002
L1847: To evaluate the impact of the COVID-19 pandemic on the primary endpoint (safety), the overview of TEAEs was repeated as a sensitivity analysis excluding all TEAEs related to COVID-19. As a supportive analysis, a summary table of the TEAEs associated with COVID-19 was reported for subjects with C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE.
L1848: As supplementary analyses, all secondary endpoints (efficacy), except SGART, were assessed over time in 3-month (91-day) time windows with the first window starting on the Month 1, Day 1 Visit. The time-normalized number of HAE attacks and the time-normalized number of HAE attacks requiring on-demand therapy were also assessed from Visit Day 1 until Visit Day 31 of the Treatment Period. This was repeated for moderate and severe HAE attacks.
L1849: ## 15.2.9. Changes to the Planned Analyses, Study 3002
L1850: The Applicant did not report any major changes to the analyses described in the final statistical analysis plan (SAP; version 1.0; dated June 25, 2021).
L1851: For the secondary safety endpoints, the Applicant planned to present tables for the C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE subjects, the non-C1-INH HAE subjects, and overall. Instead, only the summary of the TEAEs were presented for the three groups above, and all other secondary safety endpoints were presented for the overall study population only.
L1852: ## 15.2.10. Interim Analyses, Study 3002
L1853: An initial interim analysis was planned to be conducted when approximately 100 subjects had 12 months of exposure to garadacimab in study 3002 alone or across all three studies (i.e., trials 2001 and 3001 and study 3002).
L1854: Results from a second interim analysis, completed after approximately 119 subjects had at least 12 months of exposure to garadacimab in study 3002 alone or across all three studies, are presented in the Applicant's Interim Clinical Study Report (dated August 10, 2023). The data cutoff dates for the second interim analysis were February 13, 2023 (for all subjects except adolescents) and May 22, 2023 (for adolescents).
L1855: Additional interim analyses were conducted on an as-needed basis to support regulatory activities. The results of the interim analyses were not intended to be used for stopping or adapting the study or adjusting the sample size.
L1856: ## 15.2.11. Data and Safety Monitoring Committee, Study 3002
L1857: An IDMC was established to monitor the safety and efficacy data generated during the study. The IDMC reviewed accumulating data from the ongoing study. No success or futility thresholds were set for IDMC reviews. The Applicant planned to continue the trial unless a safety issue that warranted study termination was confirmed. The composition, activities, analysis, responsibilities, and timing of meetings of the IDMC were outlined in the IDMC Charter.
L1858: ## 15.3. Trial 2001: A Multicenter, Randomized, Placebo-Controlled, Parallel-Arm Study To Investigate the Efficacy, Pharmacokinetics, and Safety of CSL312 in Subjects With Hereditary Angioedema
L1859: Applicant:	CSL Behring, LLC
Drug name:	Garadacimab
Indication:	Prophylaxis to prevent attacks of HAE in subjects 12 years and older
Source of information:	CSL312_2001 Clinical Study Protocol (dated May 30, 2018);
CSL312_2001 Study Report (dated May 30, 2022);
CSL312_2001 Statistical Analysis Plan (dated June 19, 2020)
Protocol number:	CSL312_2001
Clinical phase:	Phase 2
EudraCT number:	2018-000605-24
IND number:	139936
ClinicalTrial.gov identifier:	NCT03712228
Ethics:	The clinical study protocol and all amendments were approved by the independent ethics committees/institutional review boards of the participating centers. The study was carried out in accordance with the ICH of Technical Requirements for Pharmaceuticals for Human Use (ICH) Good Clinical Practice guidelines.
L1860: Applicant:	CSL Behring, LLC
Trial centers:	Australia, Canada, Germany, Israel, and the United States
Planned duration of main phase:	Treatment Period 1: 13 weeks
Treatment Period 2: 44 weeks
Trial status:	Completed
Trial start date:	October 29, 2018
Trial completion date:	October 15, 2021
Data of database lock:	December 6, 2021
L1861: ## 15.3.1. Trial Design, Trial 2001
L1862: Results from this study support the efficacy of garadacimab in preventing HAE attacks. This was a two-period (Treatment Periods 1 and 2), multicenter (10 U.S. sites and 9 ex-U.S. sites), randomized, placebo-controlled, parallel-group, dose-ranging, phase 2 trial among 38 adult subjects (20 to 65 years of age) with C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE or FXII/PLG (Type III) HAE. Treatment Period 1 was the randomized (1:1:1:1), double-blind, placebo-controlled, dose-ranging portion of the study, and Treatment Period 2 was an open-label portion (see Figure 65). Before entering the trial, subjects with Type I or Type II HAE had to demonstrate \ge 4 HAE attacks over a consecutive 2-month period during the 3 months prior to Screening.
L1863: Figure 65. Trial Design, Trial 2001
L1864: Source: Study Report (dated May 30, 2022), Figure 9-1, p. 22
Abbreviations: C1-INH, C1 esterase inhibitor; CSL312, garadacimab; FXII, Factor XII; HAE, hereditary angioedema; PLG, plasminogen; q2wk, once every 2 weeks; q4wk, once every 4 weeks
L1865: Subjects who provided written informed consent were screened for their eligibility to enter the Run-in Period. Screening was completed within 28 days before the first day of the Run-in Period.
L1866: After Screening, eligible subjects entered the Run-in Period, which lasted a minimum of 4 weeks and up to 8 weeks, to confirm their underlying disease status and to assess their eligibility for participation in Treatment Period 1. Subjects were not permitted to use routine prophylaxis to prevent HAE attacks during the Run-in Period, but subjects could use on-demand HAE therapy (i.e., C1-INH replacement or icatibant or ecallantide) to treat emerging HAE attacks. Subjects completed the Run-in Period and could begin Treatment Period 1 when the following criteria were met:
L1867: - Subjects with C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE must have experienced \ge 2 HAE attacks within a consecutive 4-week period during the Run-in Period.
- Subjects with FXII/PLG (Type III) HAE must have experienced \ge 1 HAE attack during the Run-in Period.
L1868: Subjects entered Treatment Period 1 no later than 14 days after the Week 9 (Day 57) telephone contact during the Run-in Period. If this was not possible for the subject, then approval was required from the Applicant.
L1869: In Treatment Period 1, 32 subjects were randomized 1:1:1:1 to receive 75 mg, 200 mg, or 600 mg garadacimab SC every 4 weeks for 13 weeks, or matched placebo. Additional subjects (N=6) were assigned to 400 mg garadacimab SC Q2W for 13 weeks (open-label). Subjects documented HAE attack onset, symptoms, and use of on-demand medications in an eDiary. During each visit, the Investigator reviewed the eDiary and documented HAE attacks.
L1870: Outside of the planned randomization algorithm, six subjects with Type III HAE were enrolled. These six subjects consisted of three subjects with FXII mutation and three with PLG mutation. Once the FXII/PLG (Type III) HAE subjects satisfied all Run-in Period criteria, they were directly assigned to receive 600 mg garadacimab SC every 4 weeks (open-label).
L1871: All subjects who participated in Treatment Period 1 were able to participate in Treatment Period 2, which lasted 44 weeks and was extended up until another garadacimab study was opened for subjects to join or until the current study was discontinued. In Treatment Period 2 (open-label), subjects either continued on their regimen (if they were already assigned to 200 mg or 600 mg garadacimab SC every 4 weeks in Treatment Period 1) or were moved to the 200 mg and 600 mg garadacimab arms. After the first planned interim analysis, it was determined that there was no difference in efficacy between the 200 mg and 600 mg garadacimab arms, but there were significantly more injection site reactions in the 600 mg garadacimab arm. Therefore, a protocol amendment in March 2020 reassigned all Type I and Type II HAE subjects to 200 mg garadacimab every 4 weeks. Subjects with Type III HAE continued on 600 mg garadacimab every 4 weeks.
L1872: The overall design was generally reasonable for a proof of concept, dose-ranging trial in a development program for an HAE prophylactic therapy.
L1873: ## 15.3.2. Schedule of Assessments, Trial 2001
L1874: In Treatment Period 1 of trial 2001, subjects received garadacimab 75 mg or 200 mg or 600 mg or placebo SC every 4 weeks or 400 mg Q2W for 3 months. During Treatment Period 2 (open-label), subjects received garadacimab 600 mg or 200 mg SC every 4 weeks. Study visits, vitals, physical exams, laboratory monitoring, and AE monitoring are shown in Table 111, Table 112, Table 113, and Table 114. The laboratory analyses were conducted at a central lab. The schedules of assessments are acceptable for a phase 2, dose-ranging trial.
