L1923: ## 15.3.3. Eligibility Criteria, Trial 2001
L1924: Key inclusion and exclusion criteria for this trial are summarized below. These criteria appear reasonable for a phase 2 HAE trial.
L1925: ## **Key Inclusion Criteria**
L1926: - Adults 18 to 65 years of age.
- A diagnosis of C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE or FXII/PLG (Type III) HAE as demonstrated by clinical history consistent with HAE (SC or mucosal, nonpruritic swelling episodes without accompanying urticaria) and supportive laboratory values:C1-INH antigen concentration or function activity &lt;50% of the lower limit of the reference range and C4 antigen concentration below the lower limit of the reference range (for subjects with Type I or Type II HAE).
- HAE-associated FXII or PLG gene mutation (for subjects with Type III HAE).
- For baseline HAE attack rate:Four or more HAE attacks over a consecutive 2-month period during the 3 months before Screening, as documented in the subject's medical record (for subjects with Type I or Type II HAE).
- One or more HAE attack during the 3 months before screening, as documented in the subject's medical record (for subjects with Type III HAE).
- For subjects on prophylactic HAE therapy, documentation of baseline HAE attack rate prior to prophylactic HAE therapy was accepted.
- Willing to cease the use of C1-INH products, androgens, or antifibrinolytics for routine prophylaxis against HAE attacks on the first day of the Run-in Period, after being assessed by the investigator to be able to adequately manage on-demand treatments of HAE attacks without assistance.
L1927: ## **Key Exclusion Criteria**
L1928: - History of clinically significant arterial or venous thrombosis, or current clinically significant prothrombotic risk (including presence of a central venous access device).
- History of uncontrolled, abnormal bleeding events due to a coagulopathy, or a current clinically significant coagulopathy, or clinically significant risks for bleeding events.
- Had any preplanned surgeries during the trial that had an inherent clinically significant risk for thrombotic events or bleeding.
- Had an incurable malignancy.
- For subjects with Type I or Type II HAE, a clinically significant history of poor response to C1-INH therapy for the management of HAE.
- Female subjects with Type I or Type II HAE who initiated or changed dose of any hormonal contraceptive regimen or hormone replacement therapy (i.e., estrogen/progesterone products) within 3 months before Screening.
L1929: - Known hypersensitivity to the investigational product or any of its excipients.
L1930: ## 15.3.4. Primary and Secondary Objectives and Endpoints, Trial 2001
L1931: ## Primary Objectives and Endpoints
L1932: The primary objective of this study was to evaluate the dose and efficacy of garadacimab in the prevention of HAE attacks in subjects with C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE. The primary endpoint was the time-normalized number of investigator-confirmed HAE attacks per month during the blinded, placebo-controlled portion of the study (Treatment Period 1). The primary endpoint was calculated per subject as:
L1933: \text{[number of HAE attacks} \div \text{length of subject's evaluation period in days]} \times 30.4375.
L1934: A subject's evaluation period depended on whether the subject received an IV loading dose. All 32 subjects with Type I or Type II HAE who were assigned to 75 mg, 200 mg, or 600 mg garadacimab, or matched placebo, in Treatment Period 1 received an IV loading dose. For these subjects, their evaluation periods were from Day 6, or the date of first SC administration of the investigational product, until Day 91 or the Study Completion Visit date (whichever occurred first).
L1935: The additional six subjects who were assigned to 400 mg garadacimab Q2W (open-label) in Treatment Period 1 did not receive an IV loading dose. For these subjects, their evaluation periods were from Day 15, or the date of second SC administration of the investigational product, until Day 85 or the Study Completion Visit date (whichever occurred first).
L1936: ## Secondary Objectives and Endpoints
L1937: The secondary objectives of this study included evaluation of the efficacy of garadacimab in subjects with C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE. The secondary efficacy endpoints included:
L1938: - Number and percentage of subjects with Type I or Type II HAE who had a \ge 50\% reduction in the time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month during Treatment Period 1 compared to the Run-in Period.
- Number and percentage of subjects with Type I or Type II HAE who were attack-free during Treatment Period 1.
- Number, time-normalized number (per month), and percentage of mild, moderate, and severe HAE attacks in subjects with Type I or Type II HAE during Treatment Period 1.
- Number, time-normalized number (per month), and percentage of mild, moderate, and severe HAE attacks treated with on-demand therapy in subjects with Type I or Type II HAE during Treatment Period 1.
L1939: ## 15.3.5. Statistical Analysis Plan, Trial 2001
L1940: The final statistical analysis plan (SAP; version 2.0) is dated June 19, 2020. The analysis sets defined by the Applicant for this study included the following:
L1941: **Screening Population:** All subjects who provided written informed consent and underwent any study screening procedure.
L1942: **ITT Population:** All subjects who provided informed consent, underwent any study screening procedure, and was assigned to treatment in Treatment Period 1 or to treatment in Treatment Period 2, regardless of whether they received the investigational product. The ITT Analysis Set was analyzed using the treatment to which the subject was assigned in Treatment Period 1, in Treatment Period 2 at Week 13, or 200 mg after dose reduction from 600 mg following Amendment 2 in Treatment Period 2 (only applicable for C1-INH [Type I or Type II] HAE subjects), regardless of the actual treatment received. This was the main set used for efficacy analyses. Subjects with FXII/PLG (Type III) HAE were not included.
L1943: **Safety Population:** All subjects who provided informed consent, were assigned to treatment in Treatment Period 1 or to treatment in Treatment Period 2 and received at least 1 dose or partial dose of the investigational product. The Safety Population was based on the actual treatment received and was the main set used for safety analyses.
L1944: **Per Protocol Population:** All subjects in the ITT Analysis Set, excluding subjects who had a significant protocol violation. Protocol violations leading to exclusion from the Per Protocol Population were reviewed and agreed upon prior to conducting each of the interim analyses and before database lock.
L1945: ## 15.3.6. Analysis of the Primary Endpoint, Trial 2001
L1946: The primary analysis tested for a difference in the time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month during Treatment Period 1 between 200 mg garadacimab versus placebo and between 600 mg garadacimab versus placebo at a full alpha level of 5%. A two-sided Mann-Whitney test was used for both comparisons (each at \alpha=2.5\% to account for multiple testing). An exploratory Mann-Whitney test was conducted to compare 200 mg garadacimab and 600 mg garadacimab at \alpha=5\%. No testing was done for the 75 mg and 400 mg garadacimab arms.
L1947: ## **Handling of Missing Data**
L1948: While strategies for addressing missing data were prespecified in the final SAP, no missing data occurred for the primary endpoint. Therefore, no missing data or sensitivity analyses were conducted by the Applicant.
L1949: ## **Subgroup Analyses**
L1950: Subgroup analyses for the primary endpoint were not prespecified in the protocol or final SAP or submitted by the Applicant.
L1951: ## 15.3.7. Analysis of the Secondary Endpoint, Trial 2001
L1952: All secondary endpoints in Section 15.3.3 were investigated exploratorily via descriptive statistics. No statistical testing was conducted for these endpoints.
L1953: ## 15.3.8. Data and Safety Monitoring Committee, Trial 2001
L1954: An IDMC was established to monitor safety, efficacy, and PK/PD data generated during the trial. The IDMC was comprised of independent clinical specialists in HAE with experience in clinical trials. The IDMC reviewed accumulating data from the ongoing study in an unblinded manner. No safety concerns were identified during the IDMC meetings, and the IDMC recommended that the study continue as planned.
L1955: ## 15.3.9. Sample Size Considerations, Trial 2001
L1956: The Applicant determined that seven subjects with C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE assigned to each tested treatment arm (200 mg garadacimab, 600 mg garadacimab, or placebo) were needed to reach about 82% power for the comparisons of 200 mg garadacimab versus placebo and 600 mg garadacimab versus placebo in Treatment Period 1, under the following assumptions:
L1957: - Alpha=2.5% for each comparison using two-sided Mann-Whitney tests.
- The time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month was 2.9 in the placebo arm (derived from a previous HAE trial [3001] conducted by the Applicant, where the lower bound of the distribution-free 95% CI for the median in the first placebo Treatment Period was used).
- The time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month was 0.6 in the garadacimab arms (derived from a previous HAE trial [3001] conducted by the Applicant, where the upper bound of the distribution-free 95% CI for the median of the combined active Treatment Periods was used).
- The time-normalized HAE attack rates follow a Poisson distribution, where the lower and upper bounds of the 95% CIs for the medians are used as the rate parameters.
- Randomization allocation is equal.
L1958: The 75 mg garadacimab arm was included for PK/PD reasons and was not expected to yield maximum clinical efficacy. The sample size of seven subjects with Type I or Type II HAE for this arm was not driven by an efficacy comparison or power calculation, but to maintain study blinding and consistency with the other treatment arms. No testing for efficacy was planned for the 75 mg garadacimab arm versus placebo.
L1959: An additional 1 subject with Type I or Type II HAE was planned to be added to each of the four treatment arms above to account for dropouts during Treatment Period 1, resulting in eight subjects with Type I or Type II HAE planned per treatment arm. A total of 32 subjects with Type I or Type II HAE were randomized to the blinded, placebo-controlled portion of the study (Treatment Period 1).
L1960: An additional eight subjects with Type I or Type II HAE were planned to be assigned to 400 mg garadacimab Q2W (open-label) and an additional 10 subjects with FXII/PLG (Type III) HAE were planned to be assigned to 600 mg garadacimab every 4 weeks (open-label). A power calculation was not performed for these two treatment arms; they were included for informational purposes only and were not planned to be tested against placebo. In the study,
L1961: there were six subjects with Type I or Type II HAE in the 400 mg garadacimab arm (open-label) and six subjects with Type III HAE in the 600 mg garadacimab arm (open-label).
L1962: Overall, a total of up to 40 subjects with Type I or Type II HAE were planned to be assigned to treatment in Treatment Period 1. In the study, a total of 38 subjects with Type I or Type II HAE were enrolled.
L1963: Treatment Period 2 was open-label and comprised subjects from Treatment Period 1. Therefore, no sample size calculations were performed for Treatment Period 2.
L1964: ## 15.3.10. Protocol Amendments, Trial 2001
L1965: The original protocol (dated May 30, 2018) had two substantial amendments: Amendment 1 (dated December 18, 2018) and Amendment 2 (dated March 20, 2020).
L1966: Amendment 1 included updates to pregnancy testing, acceptable methods of contraception and use of routine (long-term) HAE prophylaxis, and the duration of the Follow-up Period (extended to 85 days), and clarifications were made to the statistical methods (before database lock).
L1967: In Amendment 2, all subjects with C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE receiving 600 mg garadacimab in Treatment Period 2 had their doses decreased to 200 mg, and corresponding edits were made to the Statistical Analyses and Methods section of the protocol (before database lock).
L1968: Additionally, Contingency Amendment 1 (dated April 9, 2020) was produced in response to the COVID-19 pandemic, which allowed all subjects in Treatment Period 2 to substitute a research center visit with a remote telephone or video conference.
L1969: ## 15.3.11. Changes to the Planned Analyses, Trial 2001
L1970: The original SAP (SAP; version 1.0; dated April 1, 2020) was modified to include updates to align with Amendment 2 and Contingency Amendment 1 of the original protocol (described above). All changes are reflected in the final SAP (version 2.0; dated June 19, 2020) and were generally reasonable.
L1971: ## 16. Efficacy
L1972: ## 16.1. Durability of Treatment Effect, Study 3002
L1973: A total of 161 subjects (151 adults and 10 adolescents) were enrolled in an open-label extension study (3002), of which 90 (56%) were garadacimab-naïve (69 new subjects and 21 subjects from trial 3001) and 71 (44%) were previously treated with garadacimab (35 subjects from trial 2001 and 36 subjects from trial 3001). Of the 161 subjects, 145 (90%) had Type I HAE, 14 (9%) had Type II HAE, and 2 (1%) had Type III HAE. The mean duration of garadacimab treatment was 14 months (range: 3 to 21 months), with 119 subjects (74%) having received garadacimab for \ge 12 months. As shown in Table 115, the mean time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month was 0.16 (95% CI=0.1, 0.22), which is generally consistent with the primary analysis
L1974: results from the pivotal study (Table 10). This treatment effect was sustained in 55 subjects (34%) who received garadacimab for &gt;15 months (mean=0.15; 95% CI=0.05, 0.25). However, because of the lack of a control arm and blinding, these results should be interpreted with caution.

L1978: ## 16.2. Results of Statistical Analyses, Trial 2001
L1979: This section summarizes subject disposition, baseline demographics and clinical characteristics, and the primary and secondary efficacy results.
L1980: ## **Subject Disposition**
L1981: Disposition information is provided in Figure 66. A total of 54 subjects were screened, of which 2 were screen failures due to not having a clinical diagnosis of C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE and reasons related to use of contraception or hormone replacement therapy. Of the 52 subjects who entered the Run-in Period, 14 failed to meet all Run-in Period completion criteria and were not assigned to treatment. A total of 38 subjects with Type I or Type II HAE were randomized in Treatment Period 1, and 6 additional subjects with FXII/PLG (Type III) HAE received 600 mg garadacimab once every 4 weeks (open-label). All 38 subjects on blinded treatment completed Treatment Period 1.
L1982: Overall, the subject disposition is generally reasonable for a phase 2 HAE trial. For further information regarding the Type III HAE subjects, see Section 6.3.2.

L1986: ## Baseline Demographics and Clinical Characteristics
L1987: The demographics and clinical characteristics of the ITT set at baseline are summarized in Table 116. Imbalances in age, sex, race, and ethnicity are noted across the treatment arms. Due to the small size of the trial (HAE is a rare condition), these imbalances are expected. This trial was only powered to detect a difference between 200 mg garadacimab versus placebo and between 600 mg garadacimab versus placebo; as such, a sample size of seven to eight subjects per treatment arm was established (see Section 15.3.8 for more details). The baseline demographics and clinical characteristics of the subjects in this study are generally reflective of the overall HAE population and are generally reasonable for a phase 2 HAE trial.

L1993: ## **Primary Efficacy Results**
L1994: The primary efficacy endpoint was the time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month during Treatment Period 1. This endpoint is consistent with the phase 3 trial (3001) and previous programs for HAE prophylaxis. The calculation of the time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month during Treatment Period 1 is provided in Section 15.3.3.
L1995: The primary efficacy results are shown in **Table 117**. The mean time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month during Treatment Period 1 was lowest in the 200 mg garadacimab arm (mean=0.05; 95% CI: -0.06, 0.15) compared to placebo (mean=4.24; 95% CI: 2.74, 5.75), with a reduction in the mean of 98.9% relative to placebo. The mean time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month during Treatment Period 1 was also considerably lower in the 600 mg garadacimab arm (mean=0.35; 95% CI: -0.02, 0.73) compared to placebo (mean=4.24; 95% CI: 2.74, 5.75), with a reduction in the mean of 91.7% relative to placebo. These results signify an improvement in both the 200 mg and 600 mg garadacimab arms compared to placebo. Further, there was a statistically significant difference (Mann-Whitney p-value &lt;0.001) in the rank sums of the time-normalized number of HAE attacks per month in the 200 mg garadacimab and placebo arms (i.e., the rank sum in the 200 mg garadacimab arm was significantly lower than the placebo arm, assuming that a higher rank indicates a worse outcome). A similar conclusion can be made for the 600 mg garadacimab arm versus placebo (Mann-Whitney p-value=0.001).
L1996: Finally, in an exploratory analysis, there was no statistical difference between the 200 mg and 600 mg garadacimab arms (Mann-Whitney p-value=0.085).

L2001: ## **Secondary Efficacy Results**

All garadacimab arms outperformed placebo on this endpoint. All subjects in the 75 mg, 200 mg, and 400 mg garadacimab arms had a reduction of at least 50%. Most subjects (86%) in the 600 mg garadacimab arm also met this endpoint. No subjects on placebo achieved a \ge 50\% reduction.

L2016: **Table 120** shows the number and percentage of mild, moderate, and severe HAE attacks among subjects with Type I or Type II HAE during Treatment Period 1, in addition to the time-normalized number of moderate and severe HAE attacks per month. There was a total of 117 HAE attacks during Treatment Period 1, of which 95 (81%), 7 (6%), and 1 (1%) were experienced by subjects on placebo, 600 mg garadacimab, and 200 mg garadacimab, respectively. Of the 117 HAE attacks, 39 (33%) were mild, 57 (49%) were moderate, and 21 (18%) were severe, with most of the moderate and severe HAE attacks occurring in the placebo
L2017: arm. Among the blinded arms, the fewest HAE attacks occurred among subjects on 200 mg garadacimab (one moderate attack from one subject) and 600 mg garadacimab (two mild attacks from two subjects, four moderate attacks from two subjects, and one severe attack from one subject). Furthermore, the mean time-normalized number of moderate and severe HAE attacks per month were considerably lower in all the garadacimab arms compared to placebo, with the 200 mg garadacimab arm yielding the most favorable results.

L2024: Abbreviations: CI, confidence interval; HAE, hereditary angioedema; ITT, intent-to-treat; max, maximum; min, minimum; N, number of subjects in the ITT Analysis Set; n, number of occurrences; q2wk, once every 2 weeks; q4wk, once every 4 weeks
L2025: The number and percentage of mild, moderate, and severe HAE attacks requiring on-demand therapy among subjects with Type I or Type II HAE during Treatment Period 1 is presented in **Table 121**, in addition to the time-normalized number of moderate and severe HAE attacks per month requiring on-demand therapy. A total of 104/117 HAE attacks (89%) required on-demand therapy during Treatment Period 1, of which 89 (86%), 3 (3%), and 1 (1%) were experienced by subjects on placebo, 600 mg garadacimab, and 200 mg garadacimab, respectively. Of the 104 HAE attacks requiring on-demand therapy, 29 (28%) were mild, 54 (52%) were moderate, and 21 (20%) were severe, with most of the moderate and severe HAE attacks requiring on-demand therapy occurring in the placebo arm. Among the blinded arms, the fewest HAE attacks requiring
L2026: on-demand therapy occurred in the 200 mg garadacimab arm (one moderate attack from one subject) and the 600 mg garadacimab arm (two moderate attacks from one subject and one severe attack from one subject). Furthermore, the mean time-normalized number of moderate and severe HAE attacks per month requiring on-demand therapy was considerably lower in all the garadacimab arms compared to placebo, with the 200 mg garadacimab arm yielding the most favorable results.

L2034: The primary and secondary efficacy results reported above support the Applicant's further investigation of the 200 mg garadacimab regimen in the phase 3 trial (3001) and provide additional supportive evidence of the effectiveness of garadacimab in preventing HAE attacks.
L2035: ## 16.2.1. Interim Analyses, Trial 2001
L2036: The first planned interim analysis was conducted after all C1-INH (Type I or Type II) HAE subjects who received 75 mg, 200 mg, or 600 mg garadacimab every 4 weeks, or matched placebo, completed Treatment Period 1. Confirmatory tests of 200 mg garadacimab versus placebo and 600 mg garadacimab versus placebo were conducted. All efficacy results in Section 16.2 and safety assessments are based on the first interim analysis.
L2037: The second planned interim analysis was conducted after all subjects with Type I or Type II HAE assigned to receive 400 mg garadacimab Q2W and all subjects with FXII/PLG (Type III) HAE who were assigned to receive 600 mg garadacimab every 4 weeks completed Treatment Period 1. This interim analysis included subjects with all HAE subtypes (Type I, Type II, and Type III). These data are not shown as all the results are open-label.
L2038: The interim analyses above were planned to provide information on safety, efficacy, and PK/PD parameters for Type I, Type II, and Type III HAE.
L2039: ## 17. Clinical Safety
L2040: ## 17.1. Subgroup Analyses, Trial 3001
L2041: The review team conducted additional safety analyses by sex, age, race and geography using system organ class and preferred terms to assess for potential safety imbalances. These post hoc subgroup analyses conducted on a small population have limited interpretability. But overall, no imbalance was identified.

L2088: ## 22. Data Integrity-Related Consults (Office of Scientific Investigations, Other Inspections)
L2089: For this BLA 761367, two clinical investigators, Drs Joshua Jacobs and Timothy Craig, and the Applicant CSL Behring, LLC were inspected, covering two clinical studies: trial 3001 and study 3002. Based on the inspections, the studies appeared to have been adequately conducted, and the data generated by the clinical investigator sites and submitted by the Applicant appeared acceptable in support of this BLA.
L2090: During the clinical investigator inspections, the source records related to the primary efficacy endpoint and HAE attack characteristics (attack location, attack severity, on-demand therapies used (if any) and trigger for attack) were reviewed and verified against the Applicant's line listings for the subjects. The inspections did not identify discrepancies related to the documentation of the number of HAE attacks, which served as the basis for the primary efficacy endpoint.
L2091: During Dr Jacob's clinical site inspection, several discrepancies pertaining to the location of the HAE attack and attack trigger were identified when comparing the source documents with the Applicant's line listings. Some of the discrepancies were due to a limitation within the case report form and the electronic data capture system, which only allowed three entries per anatomical location. As a result, some of the attack locations were grouped into general body areas. See the Clinical Inspection Summary dated August 22, 2024, for additional details. The discrepancies related to the attack location and trigger did not affect the primary efficacy endpoint.
L2092: ## 23. Labeling: Key Changes and Considerations
L2093: This PI review includes a high-level summary of the rationale for major changes to the finalized PI as compared to the Applicant's draft PI (Table 126). The PI was reviewed to ensure that PI meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent with labeling guidance, conveys clinically meaningful and scientifically accurate information needed for the safe and effective use of the drug, and provides clear and concise information for the healthcare practitioner.
L2094: **Table 126. Key Labeling Changes and Considerations**
L2095: Full PI Sections1	Rationale for Major Changes to FDA Revised PI2 Compared to Applicant's Draft PI
1 INDICATIONS AND USAGE	
No changes – the indication statement is consistent with other approved product labeling for HAE prophylactic therapies
The recommended dosage of ANDEMBRY is an initial loading dose of 400 mg (two 200 mg injections) administered subcutaneously on the first day of treatment followed by a maintenance dose of 200 mg administered subcutaneously every month.

2 DOSAGE AND ADMINISTRATION	
A preparation and administration instructions subsection were added to Section 2 to provide healthcare providers with instructions for the use of ANDEMBRY, which is intended for self-administration by the patient and/or caregiver. Therefore, the HCP are instructed to [REDACTED] (b) (4) patients for subcutaneous injection technique.

4 CONTRAINDICATIONS	None – no changes
5 WARNINGS AND PRECAUTIONS	
[REDACTED] (b) (4)
The proposed label lacked information regarding the number of adult and adolescent subjects who had been exposed to garadacimab, specifically subjects who had served as the basis for the safety evaluation. The review team added language that the Clinical Trials Experience subsection reflects the safety of ANDEMBRY from the exposure of 161 adult and adolescent patients aged 12 years and older who received at least one dose of ANDEMBRY 200 mg subcutaneously.

6 ADVERSE REACTIONS	[REDACTED] (b) (4) injection site reactions, which occurred more frequently in the placebo arm in trial 3001 (VANGUARD), as an adverse reaction. A revised adverse drugs reaction table included TEAEs that occurred with ≥7% frequency with garadacimab treatment and greater than placebo.
A new subheading for prolonged coagulation lab parameters was added to convey that abnormal aPTT and PT findings were observed in trial 3001 but were not associated with increased risk of bleeding, as this may be useful information for providers. Given that the lab tests are not clinically significant findings, the results were not added to the ADR table, but as a separate lab test subheading. This information was included in Section 6 rather than 12 because it was observed too infrequently to be considered a PD finding.
L2096: Full PI Sections1	Rationale for Major Changes to FDA Revised PI2 Compared to Applicant's Draft PI
7 DRUG INTERACTIONS	A Drug-Laboratory test interaction is provided because ANDEMBRY can prolong activated partial thromboplastin time (aPTT). The reagents used in the aPTT laboratory test initiates an intrinsic coagulation through the activation of FXII, inhibiting plasma FXIIa by ANDEMBRY and can prolong aPTT in the assay.
8 USE IN SPECIFIC POPULATIONS (e.g., Pregnancy, Lactation, Females and Males of Reproductive Potential, Pediatric Use, Geriatric Use, Renal Impairment, Hepatic Impairment)	Exposure numbers in pediatric and geriatric patients were revised to reflect those in trial 3001; [REDACTED] (b) (4). Although exposure in geriatric patients was limited, the review team concluded that there were no differences in safety/effectiveness by age, consistent with other approved product labels.
10 OVERDOSAGE	This section is removed and omitted from the PI because no specific overdosage data were available. [REDACTED] (b) (4)
12 CLINICAL PHARMACOLOGY	Observed PK data following a single 200 mg dose was added as it included rich PK sampling from the largest sample size across the Applicant's clinical program.
Language was added to describe garadacimab exposure in adolescent patients relative to adult patients.
13 NONCLINICAL TOXICOLOGY	Language was added to state that animal studies were not conducted to evaluate the carcinogenic or mutagenic potential of garadacimab-xxxx.
Language for fertility and reproductive performance was added based on animal data from mature male and female rabbits.

14 CLINICAL STUDIES	New table inserted with patient demographics and baseline disease characteristics
Table 4 revised and [REDACTED] (b) (4)
[REDACTED] (b) (4) was removed as this information is duplicative with the primary endpoint.
Result for the prespecified secondary endpoints of number of HAE attacks treated with on-demand medication and number of moderate or severe HAE attacks were [REDACTED] (b) (4). Although not multiplicity controlled, these are clinically relevant outcomes included in other HAE product labeling.
Prespecified secondary endpoints of proportion of patients with ≥50, 70, 90, 100% reduction during Treatment Period versus run-in were considered clinically meaningful and retained as labeling claims with revisions. These labeling claims are also included in other approved HAE product labels.
[REDACTED] (b) (4)

The self-administration counseling information was included in this section for HCP to instruct [REDACTED] (b) (4) patients for self-administration of ANDEMBRY.
The strength of ANDEMBRY autoinjector was revised to reflect the total dose per volume (200 mg/1.2 mL) [REDACTED] (b) (4) consistent with Guidance for Industry: Safety Considerations for Container Labels and Carton Labeling Design to Minimize Medication Errors (May 2022).

L2176: ## 1 Executive Summary
L2177: ## 1.1 Introduction
L2178: The Sponsor is developing CSL312 (garadacimab), an activated Factor XII (FXIIa) inhibitor monoclonal antibody for prophylaxis to prevent attacks of hereditary angioedema (HAE). CSL312 has been developed as a combination product incorporating a prefilled syringe which contains 200 mg of the formulated CSL312 active substance, defined as the CSL312 semi-finished product (SFP) 200 mg. Extractables and leachables studies were conducted with the container closure system that were evaluated in the present review.
L2179: ## 1.2 Brief Discussion of Nonclinical Findings
L2180: Based on a Safety Concern Threshold (SCT) of (b)(4) \mug/day, the Analytical Evaluation Threshold (AET) for CSL312 SFP 200mg was calculated to be (b)(4) \mug/mL.
L2181: The PFS consists of (b)(4). CSL312 DP 200mg NSD contains one fixed dose for a single subcutaneous injection.
L2182: An extractable study was performed on (b)(4) rubber stopper. Eleven substances were identified at detectable levels as well as (b)(4). Compared to the qualification threshold (QT) of (b)(4) \mug/day, which is applicable to non-genotoxic compounds, only the level of (b)(4) exceeded the QT. However, in both the initial and long-term leachable studies, (b)(4) was not observed above the AET.
L2183: To assess potential leachables in CSL312 SFP 200mg (PFS), a leachable profiling study was performed on one batch (batch P100172586) shortly after its production. In addition, to monitor leachables that might arise during its shelf life, the same batch of CSL312 SFP 200mg (PFS) was also tested after storage for 34 months at (b)(4). Among the identified leachables, only the level of (b)(4) was above the QT of (b)(4) \mug/day. An assessment indicated that the level of (b)(4) does not pose a toxicological concern.
L2184: Levels of elemental (metal) leachables were also determined to pose no safety concerns.
L2185: In conclusion, the levels of leachables from the primary container closure system of CSL312 SFP 200mg do not appear to pose any safety concerns. The assessment was not ideal as only 1 batch was evaluated.
L2186: ## 1.3.2 Nonclinical Comments to Sponsor
L2187: None.

L2201: CSL312 is a fully human IgG4 monoclonal antibody comprised of two heavy and two light chains, joined by disulfide bonds. CSL312 binds to activated human coagulation factor XII (FXIIa) and inhibits its catalytic activity.

L2231: ## 2.5 Proposed Clinical Population and Dosing Regimen
L2232: The recommended dose of Garadacimab (CSL312) is an initial loading dose of 400 mg administered subcutaneously as two 200 mg injections on the first day of treatment followed by a monthly dose of 200 mg to prevent attacks of hereditary angioedema (HAE) in adult and pediatric patients (aged 12 years and older).
L2233: ## 2.6 Regulatory Background
L2234: BLA 761367 was submitted to the FDA on October 13, 2023. Upon reviewing the extractable and leachable information, one information request was sent to the Sponsor on March 25, 2024. The responses from the Sponsor were included in the current review.
L2235: 1. IR dated 03/25/2024
L2236: *We are reviewing your October 13, 2023, submission, and have the following request for information.*
L2237: *In Section 3.2.P.5.5 of your submission, Characterization of Impurities, results of extractable and leachable studies were summarized.*
L2238: - *This included an extractable study summary (Section 3.2.P.5.5-1, Document ES-505-01). However, original study reports are needed for our review (Study ID 3790041 and 4945673). Provide original study reports of extractable studies or if they were submitted, direct us to their location in the application.*
- *For each identified extractable and leachable, provide information of maximum patient exposure on a \mu\text{g}/\text{day} or \mu\text{g}/\text{dose} basis.*
L2239: *In order to facilitate the review of your submission, submit the requested information to your BLA no later than April 3, 2024.*
L2240: ## 3 Studies Submitted
L2241: ## 3.1 Studies Reviewed
L2242: The Sponsor's summaries as well as laboratory reports of the extractables and leachables studies with the primary container system were reviewed to conduct safety assessments of the levels of extractables and leachables.

L2245: ## 11 Integrated Summary and Safety Evaluation
L2246: CSL312 drug substance contains CSL312 monoclonal antibody (active ingredient) in a buffer containing 20 mM Histidine, 150 mM Arginine, 140 mM Proline and 0.02% w/v Polysorbate 80, pH 6.1.
L2247: The container-closure system for CSL312 SFP 200mg is [REDACTED]. During storage of CSL312 SFP 200mg, the liquid solution has [REDACTED] contact with the [REDACTED] container closure system including [REDACTED]. The presence of Polysorbate 80, a non-ionic detergent, in the CSL312 SFP liquid formulation, may increase the potential for materials to leach from materials in contact with the liquid.
L2248: A standalone extraction study was conducted with plunger stopper as this was considered the primary source of extractables and leachable. To assess leachables in CSL312 SFP 200mg resulting from the manufacturing process and/or storage, leachable profiling studies were performed on CSL312 SFP 200mg at an initial timepoint, and after storage of the SFP for 34 months at [REDACTED].
L2249: Per PQRI (Product Quality Research Institute) guidelines, an Analytical Evaluation Threshold (AET) for organic extractables and leachables in the CSL312 SFP 200mg was calculated to be [REDACTED] \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} using the Safety Concern Threshold (SCT) of [REDACTED].
L2250: \mu\text{g}/\text{day} based on the following equation and worst-case assumptions: 1 labeled dose per container, 2 containers per day (i.e., for the loading dose of 400 mg, two containers are used), and 1.2 mL per container.
L2251: (b) (4)
L2252: Estimated AET =
L2253: ## Extractables
L2254: An extractables study (Study # 3790041) was performed on [REDACTED] (b) (4) rubber stopper with [REDACTED] (b) (4). The extraction conditions were: 1) static extraction (PBS buffer at pH 2.5 and pH 9.5, and 50% aqueous ethanol solution (v/v) at 50^\circ\text{C} for 24 hours), analyzed by headspace gas chromatography with mass spectrometry (HS-GC-MS); 2) thermal extraction at 80^\circ\text{C} for 1 hour, analyzed by gas chromatography with flame ionization detection and mass spectrometry (GC-FID/MS), gas chromatography with flame ionization detection (GCFID) ([REDACTED] (b) (4)), high-performance liquid chromatography with diode array detector and mass spectrometry (HPLC-DAD/MS), mass spectrometry with inductively coupled plasma (ICP-MS); and 3) Soxhlet extraction performed with methanol, analyzed by gas chromatography with thermal energy analysis (GC-TEA) ([REDACTED] (b) (4)).
L2255: The Analytical Threshold (AT) reference solution was set to [REDACTED] (b) (4) \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}. The AT is acceptable, as it is lower than AET of [REDACTED] (b) (4) \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} and therefore is sensitive enough. To reach the final AT concentration in the samples for analysis, 10 pieces of rubber stopper were extracted with 150 ml of solvent. Eleven extractables were identified and presented in the table below.

L2269: ## Leachables
L2270: To assess potential leachables in CSL312 SFP 200mg (PFS), a leachable profiling study was performed on one batch (batch P100172586) shortly after its production (Study # 5366678). To monitor leachables that might arise during its shelf life, the same batch of CSL312 SFP 200mg (PFS) was also tested after storage for 34 months at [REDACTED]
L2271: (Study # 6379671).
L2272: In the initial profiling study (Study # 5366678) and end of shelf life study (Study #6379671), semi-volatile organic compounds were profiled by gas chromatography with flame ionization detection and mass spectrometry (GC-FID/MS), while non-volatile substances were profiled by high performance liquid chromatography with diode array detector and mass spectrometry (HPLC-DAD/MS). In addition, [REDACTED] content was profiled by transversely heated graphite atomizer – atomic adsorption spectroscopy (THGA-AAS). Elemental impurities were profiled using inductively coupled plasma mass spectrometric detection (see below). The analytical threshold (AT) for identification of leachables was set to [REDACTED] \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}. The [REDACTED] content detected with THGA-AAS corresponds to [REDACTED] \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}. The AT is acceptable, as it is lower than AET of [REDACTED] \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} and therefore is sensitive enough. In the end of shelf life/long-term storage study (Study # 6379671), the AT remained to be [REDACTED] \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}, which is acceptable.

L2311: Figure 3 illustrates the Trial Design for Trial 3001, a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel-group study. The trial consists of four main phases:
Screening: Up to 1 month. Participants must have a Screening Visit and be \ge12 years of age with type 1 or 2 HAE.
Run-in-Period: Up to 2 months. Participants must have a baseline attack rate of HAE attack \ge 1 attack/month.
Treatment Period: 6 months (SC q1M). Participants are randomized to receive either 200 mg CSL312 or Placebo. Both arms received a SC loading dose as the first dose.
Follow-up Period: 2 months. Participants proceed to the End of Treatment Visit and OLE Entry.
Flowchart illustrating the Trial Design for Trial 3001. The trial consists of four phases: Screening (Up to 1 month), Run-in-Period (Up to 2 months), Treatment Period (6 months, SC q1M), and Follow-up Period (2 months). During the Screening phase, participants must have a Screening Visit and be ≥12 years of age with type 1 or 2 HAE. During the Run-in-Period, participants must have a baseline attack rate of HAE attack ≥ 1 attack/month. During the Treatment Period, participants are randomized to receive either 200 mg CSL312 or Placebo, both with a SC loading dose as the first dose in both arms. During the Follow-up Period, participants proceed to the End of Treatment Visit and OLE Entry.

L2319: Figure 10 is a schematic diagram illustrating the Study Design for Study 3002, divided into three main periods: Requirements to enter OL Treatment Period, OL Treatment Period, and Follow-up.
Requirements to enter OL Treatment Period:
2001 & 3001 roll over subjects: No screening or run in – last Lab data collected at EoT visit and medical history from phase 2 / phase 3 sufficient.
CSL312-naïve subjects: Undergo a Screening Visit (Up to 1 month, type 1 or 2 HAE) followed by a Run-in Period (Up to 2 months, To confirm baseline HAE attack rate).
OL Treatment Period: Variable (12 months minimum). Study site visits are required. Treatment is 200 mg CSL312 every month. A Day 1 loading dose of 400 mg CSL312 is also indicated.
Follow-up: 2 months after End of Treatment Visit. Study site visits or telephone call.

L2333: Figure 24 illustrates the Study Design for Study 1001. The timeline spans from Day -21 (Screening) through Day 85 (End of Study), divided into Screening, In-house, and Follow-up periods.
Subjects were randomized on Day 1 (In-house period) to receive either CSL312 or Placebo.
The study included two main administration routes:
Intravenous Infusion: Five cohorts (IV1 to IV5) received increasing doses of CSL312 (0.1 mg/kg, 0.3 mg/kg, 1 mg/kg, 3 mg/kg, 10 mg/kg).
Subcutaneous Injection: Three cohorts (SC1 to SC3) received increasing doses of CSL312 (1 mg/kg, 3 mg/kg, 10 mg/kg).
Subjects in the IV cohorts received the infusion on Day 1. Subjects in the SC cohorts received the injection on Day 1. All subjects were followed up until Day 85.

L2371: Figure 59 displays two panels showing the relationship between simulated HAE patients (>=12 years) and AUCtau,ss (ug*hour/mL). The top panel is a scatter plot showing the Relative risk of HAE attack (Y-axis, 0.00 to 0.20) versus AUCtau,ss (X-axis, 0 to 40000). A blue curve represents the mean relative risk, and a grey shaded area represents the approximate 95% CI for the mean. A dashed red line indicates the AUCtau,ss value corresponding to a 10% relative risk of HAE attack (approximately 10,000 ug*hour/mL). The bottom panel is a density plot showing the distribution of simulated AUCtau,ss values. A boxplot is overlaid, indicating the 25th, 50th, and 75th percentiles, with whiskers extending to 1.5 times the IQR. The boxplot is centered around the 10% relative risk threshold.
L2372: Figure 62 is a schematic diagram illustrating the Study 3002 design, divided into three main phases: Requirements to enter OL Treatment Period, OL Treatment Period, and Follow-up.
Requirements to enter OL Treatment Period:
2001 & 3001 roll over subjects: No screening or run in – last Lab data collected at EoT visit and medical history from phase 2 / phase 3 sufficient.
CSL312-naïve subjects: Undergo a Screening Period (Up to 1 month) and a Run-in Period (Up to 2 months).
OL Treatment Period: Variable (12 months minimum). Subjects receive 200 mg CSL312 every month. For CSL312-naïve subjects, the initial dose is 400 mg (Day 1 loading dose) followed by 200 mg CSL312 every month.
Follow-up: 2 months after End of Treatment Visit. Subjects receive Study site visits or telephone call.
L2373: Diagram illustrating the Trial Design, Trial 2001, showing the timeline and treatment groups.
The trial consists of a Run-in Period, Treatment Period 1, Treatment Period 2, and a Follow-up Period. All subjects may use on-demand therapy to treat emergent HAE attacks during the entire study duration.
Subjects were screened for C1-INH HAE or FXII/PLG HAE.
Screened subjects with C1-INH HAE:
Placebo q4wk: Followed by 200 mg CSL312 q4wk.
75 mg CSL312 q4wk: Followed by 600 mg CSL312 q4wk.
200 mg CSL312 q4wk: Followed by 200 mg CSL312 q4wk.
600 mg CSL312 q4wk: Followed by 600 mg CSL312 q4wk.
400 mg CSL312 q2wk: Followed by 200 mg CSL312 q4wk and 600 mg CSL312 q4wk.
Screened subjects with FXII/PLG HAE:
600 mg CSL312 q4wk: Followed by 600 mg CSL312 q4wk.
L2374: Flowchart illustrating Subject Disposition, Trial 2001.
The process starts with Consented / Screened (N = 54). Screen Failures (N = 2) lead to Entered Run-in Period (N = 52). Not Assigned to Treatment (N = 14) leads to Assigned to Treatment (N = 38*).
The subjects assigned to treatment are distributed across five arms:
Placebo q4wk (N = 8): Completed TP1 (N = 8), Started TP2 (N = 8). Discontinued TP1 (N = 0).
75 mg CSL312 q4wk (N = 9): Completed TP1 (N = 9), Started TP2 (N = 9). Discontinued TP1 (N = 0).
200 mg CSL312 q4wk (N = 8): Completed TP1 (N = 8), Started TP2 (N = 8). Discontinued TP1 (N = 0).
600 mg CSL312 q4wk (N = 7): Completed TP1 (N = 7), Started TP2 (N = 7). Discontinued TP1 (N = 0).
400 mg CSL312 q2wk (N = 6): Completed TP1 (N = 6), Started TP2 (N = 6). Discontinued TP1 (N = 0).
The timeline indicates the Run-in Period and Treatment Period 1.
